# Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (Qwen4Exp) optimization ledger

- **Ledger date:** 2026-09-29 (expanded after a cross-document coverage review;
  §10 records the source inventory and experiment crosswalk)
- **Host:** Framework `gfx1151`, Radeon 8060S / Strix Halo, machine
  `55ea6c509d0b49eea8de7094a1023668`
- **Model / quant:** unsloth `Qwen3.8-Flash-Next` `UD-Q4_K_XL`, BF16 KV, warm
  PLE, chunk1024, shipped `production` profile

**This is the single open-work list for closing the prefill, decode and MTP gap
on this model, and the single registry of what has already been tried.** Every
open row names a measured cost where available, or explicitly calls for
measurement, the evidence that bounds the opportunity, the first bounded
experiment, and the gate that decides it. Rows leave the open
list only by promotion, by recorded rejection, or by a stated blocker, and every
rejection lands in §7 with the condition that would reopen it.

## Contents

- §0 How this document relates to the others
- §1 Where we stand against every engine
- §2 Arithmetic policy and kernel mechanisms
- §3 Cross-cutting decisions and measurement prerequisites
- §4 Prefill open work, by functional block
- §5 Decode open work, by functional block
- §6 MTP open work
- §7 Tried and not retained, by functional block (the registry)
- §8 Measurement rules for this model
- §9 Cadence
- §10 Coverage audit and historical experiment crosswalk

## 1. Where we stand against every engine

Author rows are opportunity indicators only; the protocol confounds C1-C6 in

MTP rows, author-reported by gufo at d0: mixed text **32.20**, repetitive text
**59.30** tok/s (llama.cpp `6fcaa16f`: 31.73 / 48.12). Our MTP is explicit,
default-off, capped at 1K context, and the retained serial provider measured
**0.7407x** true AR (§6).

**What the table says.** The same-host fixture establishes a substantial prefill
gap to pwilkin; it does not establish a controlled ratio against the later
255.066 tok/s promotion run. Gufo and halo-box PR #91 report about 1630 tok/s
under different protocols, and gufo reports 25.87 tok/s AR decode. These are
reproduction targets, not measured speedup ratios over hipEngine. `X-2` must
establish the comparison basis before choosing a performance target. Gufo is a
useful mechanism reference because it is a purpose-built engine on the same
payload; its quality checks do not qualify it under our production contract.

### 1.2 Kernel time per family, per 1K prompt tokens

Ours is the `42ce71156` attribution (§1 of STATUS) divided by four; gufo's is
its own 2026-09-21 d0 pp2048 profile (1362.8 ms kernel time in a 1380 ms span,
[EXPERIMENTS.md](https://github.com/gufo-org/gufo/blob/fee9d2a/docs/models/qwen3.8-flash-next/EXPERIMENTS.md))
divided by two. **Approximate by construction:** the prompt lengths differ
(4096 against 2048, so our attention includes sparse rows theirs does not),
the taxonomies differ, and gufo's figure is author-reported.

| Family | Ours ms / 1K | gufo ms / 1K (author-reported) | Notes |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Routed experts (gate/up + down) | 1401.7 | ~238 (35%) | Ours post-unroll 3377.1 + 2229.8 ms, including 1240 ms exact repair |
| Dense projections | 1308.7 | ~198 (29%) | Gufo reports 30-34 TFLOPS for dense F16 against its measured 59 TFLOPS ceiling |
| Hyper-connections | 631.4 | ~72 combine (10.5%) + part of ~48 (7%, HC down/inject/shared) | Different bucket boundaries |
| GDN | 205.4 | ~65 (9.5%) | Different sources and shapes |
| Attention and indexer | 350.6 | ~37 (5.5%) | Ours predates the full-attention promotion; theirs has no sparse rows at 2048 |
| MoE router/reduce | 189.6 | not separated | No paired comparison |
| **Total** | **4168** (post-unroll 16673.2 / 4) | **681** | Not a same-host speedup ratio |

**1.2 is an exploratory cross-source cost sketch, not a same-host gap table.**
It suggests investigating several families, but these costs cannot rank local
recoverable milliseconds or establish that every family is behind.

Our rows mix the pre-unroll anchor (dense, HC, GDN, attention, reduce) with
the post-unroll MoE capture; `elementwise_norm` and the remainder are omitted,
so the rows do not sum exactly to the post-unroll total.

### 1.3 Current attribution checkpoint — 2026-09-28 UTC

The [current-owner packet](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-current-attribution/README.md)
uses `2789c19d6`, the host/model/quant named above, BF16 KV, chunk1024 and
production manifest `35e57360e648bcc8ce8f21d89bf5583a0b4217061b306ec96616b2607cc6e565`.
It replaces historical costs for prioritizing short-context experiments, not
for claiming speedup. The section headings below still identify their dated
anchors; the packet supplies current symbols, roles and layer engagement.

One `code-p4096` capture attributes 10,708/10,708 launches and 15,741.963 ms
kernel union in a 16,319.888 ms request; unprofiled wall is 16,334.447 ms.
Current buckets are routed gate/up + down 5,605.754 ms, dense 4,553.007 ms,
HC 2,587.665 ms, attention 1,040.374 ms, GDN 844.295 ms, router/reduce
763.833 ms, norms 319.494 ms, indexer 17.520 ms and PLE 9.834 ms.
Thus routed experts, dense and HC remain the first bounded owner screens;
these costs do not imply additive or fully recoverable savings. F16 staging
casts total 131.001 ms, a measured ceiling for DP-5 before fusion costs.
Sparse attention is 24 launches / 455.080 ms; full attention is 36 /
438.971 ms. Do not reuse historical launch counts with these timings.

A supplemental copy trace reconciles 15,600.436 ms kernels plus 2.711 ms
observed device copies to a 15,603.147 ms interval union inside 16,159.403 ms.
Reset memset kernels are counted. HIP API host durations overlap device work;
the residual is not exclusively recoverable launch overhead.

Fixed-live4097 decode has 53.701–54.777 ms device time across three repeats.
Repeat 1 owners: MoE 24.625 ms, linear 23.121, QSA 2.594, GDN 2.386,
GR read 0.851, boundary 0.478 and PLE 0.034. Gate/up alone is 14.055 ms,
Q5_1 down 5.447 ms. Profiled wall varies 59–239 ms and unprofiled wall
124–141 ms, so serving-wrapper costs and throughput remain unqualified.
Prefill logits/manifest and decode token/state hashes agree across runs;
allocation growth and post-close live allocations are zero.

X-5 finds profiler scratch sizes 36 bytes (GDN) and 92 bytes (dense-wide).
Queue scratch allocations occur before, not during, the measured prefill.
Decode kernels report zero scratch. Metadata/disassembly and ordered
trigger/victim A/B remain necessary; neither throttling nor its absence is
proven. X-11 receives engagement evidence only, not repair-oracle/task coverage.

The [scratch metadata/pilot packet](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-scratch-pilot/README.md)
confirms 36-byte GDN and 92-byte dense-wide private segments in the cached
code objects, with scratch loads/stores in disassembly. Four fresh ordered
GDN pilots (sham/scratch/scratch/sham) preserve outputs/state and show no large
persistent penalty on the scratch-free GDN victim (after/before 0.99838–1.00057).
This is not the scratch-enabled victim test: X-5 still requires the full kernel
metadata census, dense-wide trigger, representative victims and clock/allocation
telemetry before a causal conclusion.

The [scratch-growth screen](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-scratch-growth/README.md)
then tests wave-norm GDN as a scratch-enabled victim after dense-wide. Four
fresh sham/scratch/scratch/sham processes give after/before ratios
0.99604/0.99747/0.99856/1.00402, with exact GDN output/state and a passing dense
CPU F16 reference. A separate trace confirms queue scratch grows from
1,638,400 to 3,932,160 bytes and the 36→92→36-byte dispatch order on one queue.
Boundary clock snapshots are stable. No large growth-induced penalty appears
in this synthetic screen; it does not test a pristine first-scratch transition
or representative decode victims, and X-5 remains open.

The [current code-object census](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-scratch-census/README.md)
reconciles all 70 traced project symbols against 23 current cached libraries:
only the above GDN/dense-wide symbols have nonzero private segments, and all
sizes match the traces. Two ROCm copy/fill symbols remain runtime-unresolved.
This establishes current-build metadata consistency, not historical loaded
binary identity (the captures did not hash loaded libraries). X-5 still needs
representative decode-victim and first-scratch causality evidence.

The [Q5_1 first-scratch screen](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-decode-scratch/README.md)
now verifies the real layer0 down/combine owner before and after the first GDN
scratch allocation. Four fresh sham/scratch/scratch/sham ratios are
0.97503/0.99101/0.99345/0.99916 with byte-identical outputs. The cached profiler
confirms baseline Q5_1 calls precede the first 1,638,400-byte allocation and the
36-byte GDN trigger; subsequent Q5_1 calls use the same queue. No large penalty
appears for this pair. The subsequent
[dense-wide first-trigger screen](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-decode-dense-scratch/README.md)
also shows no large persistent Q5_1 penalty: scratch ratios 0.99501/1.00294
versus sham 0.98817/0.98892, exact victim outputs and a passing dense CPU
reference. Its trace verifies the first 3,932,160-byte allocation follows
baseline Q5_1 calls and precedes the 92-byte dense-wide trigger, with no GDN
execution. Gate/up and Q8 decode victims remain uncovered; no broad
throttle-clearance or speedup claim follows.


## 2. Arithmetic policy and kernel mechanisms

### 2.1 What the fast engines compute

Read from source at gufo `fee9d2a`
(`src/models/qwen38_flash_next/kernels/rocm/executor.cpp`, `kernels.hip.cpp`),
halo-box PR #91 / #87 / #104 descriptions and pwilkin `40a9f4d01`:

| Block | gufo (prefill) | halo-box / pwilkin | hipEngine production default |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Dense Q8_0 projections | `DenseF16GEMMKernel`: Q8_0 codes decoded to F16 in LDS (exact magic-number construction), **F16 activation rows**, F16 WMMA, F32 accumulate, at **every layer** for shapes with ≥2048 outputs and K ≤ 2560; the other Q8_0 shapes run `W8A8BlockedWmmaGEMMKernel` (int8 WMMA over activations quantized per 32-block) | `mmb.cu`: BF16 weights and BF16 activations, BF16 WMMA, all layers | F16 operands (`dense_wide256`) at layers 16-47 only; **exact F32 coltile chain at 0-15** |
| Routed experts | `RoutedF16GEMMKernel`: Q4_K / Q5_1 / Q8_0 decoded to F16 in LDS, F16 activations, F16 WMMA; gate/up paired with a SwiGLU epilogue; 48/64/128-row expert tiles picked from the real bucket counts; **no repair pass** | MMB routed GLU (BF16), Q4_K two-lane decode | iu8 WMMA with at-risk flags plus a **sparse exact repair** (1240 ms per `code-p4096`) |
| Hyper-connections | `HcDownF16Gemm` (int8 WMMA from a tiled-Q8 copy of the norm) plus `HcMixF16Gemm` (Q8 → F16 up projection with the mix and inject in the epilogue); combine fused with the MoE epilogue and the next norm | fused gate GEMM + mix, bf16 streams end to end, combine+norm computes the next inject | exact F32 `q8_0_gr_up_sigmoid_mean_coltile2_branch4_rowbatch4` (2507 ms, 256x weight re-read) |
| Router and small F32 weights | hipBLAS SGEMM (router and shared-expert gate in one GEMM) | activations rounded to F16 on WMMA ("every F32 weight of these GGUFs is F16-exact") | exact F32 tile4 producer |
| Attention | `WmmaCausalAttentionKernel`: **F16 K/V cache**, WMMA QK and PV, four queries x twelve heads packed over the union of their selections | HS256 WMMA flash attention; WMMA indexer scoring | BF16 KV; scalar exact FMA sparse attention (`__fmul_rn` with a contraction barrier) |
| GDN | F32 row-split recurrence, four tokens staged in LDS, three windows of loads in flight | chunked fp16 WMMA chunk scan (NMSE ~2e-7) with fused conv/SiLU, q/k norm and gated RMS norm | exact F32 register-state / tile-16 recurrence |
| Activation conversion | producers write the F16 (and tiled Q8) copy their consumer reads; no separate conversion pass on the wide route | producers write the BF16 copy themselves | one bounded staging cast per consumer set (`HIPENGINE_GGUF_Q8_DENSE_WIDE_F16_ACT`) |
| Prefill chunk | 2048 | `-ub 4096` in the published benchmarks | 1024 |

Their quality evidence is not our gate. gufo's is consistency (batched against
isolated, chunk boundaries, snapshot replay, 63 tg128 requests matching fresh
AR) plus an FP64 attention-operator check; its own scalar-versus-bulk prefill
comparison reports "same top-1; logit RMSE 0.18, not bit-identical". halo-box
uses PPL and KLD against its own reference build. Neither is measured against a
strict F32 teacher at our calibrated envelope.

### 2.2 What that means for us

1. **The historical arithmetic recovery substantially reduced throughput.** The
   September 14 recovery (`20260914T090826`, `49ffa3cb5`) zeroed fifteen
   unqualified arithmetic selectors after the old composition measured max KL
   `0.0546`. Same host, same residency, counterbalanced: PP
   **292.7 / 309.3 / 285.8 → 172.9 / 179.7 / 130.0** (−41 / −42 / −55%) at
   p512 / 1K / 4K and TG **20.2 / 19.4 / 18.1 → 17.6 / 17.0 / 11.4**. Q8 and QSA
   were then partly restored under qualified variants, and the September 16-28
   promotions have brought p4096 from 130.0 to ~255 tok/s — still below the
   285.8 of the unqualified composition.
2. **The reviewed competitors use reduced-precision operands for their large
   matrix paths.** Our F16-operand `dense_wide256` passes its local numerical
   gate at 16-47; the sibling WMMA route failed at 0-47 by 10% of the mean-KL
   budget (1.099e-3 against 1e-3). That is not a direct wide-route 0-47 verdict. The
   router can amplify small logit changes near expert-selection ties (§8 rule 6).
   This is a hypothesis to localize, not proof of a router
   defect or that every reduction change fails. Check `flip_eligible_rows`,
   routed expert choices and the first divergent boundary before assigning
   cause; the full production gate remains binding.
3. **So there are two tracks, and they must be ranked separately.**
   - **Track A, kernel technology at our arithmetic.** Arithmetic-preserving
     changes (LDS-staged weights, epilogue fusion, larger chunks, producer
     publication) are cheaper to admit and are ranked in §4-§5.
   - **Track B, arithmetic class.** Moving more of the model to the
     competitors' operand classes is the larger prize and the harder gate. It
     is informed by `X-1`: compare competitor outputs with our strict teacher
     while testing bounded local candidates. A competitor pass does not admit
     our implementation, and a competitor failure does not rule out a scoped
     candidate. `X-1` is an information priority, not a blanket build blocker.

| ID | Item | Evidence | First bounded experiment | State |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| X-1 | **Competitor arithmetic and local error localization.** Preserve packet `E10` separately from whole-model comparison | STATUS §2.6 supplies only a simulated BF16 bound; no competitor has a same-teacher full-model certificate | First score the already-captured identical-operand MMB packet against F64 using the replay bridge. Separately align exact token IDs, vocabulary, KV policy and teacher-forced contexts for the canonical 12 cases plus heldouts; report mean/p95/p99/max KL and category top-1. A pass or failure describes that engine/configuration only; local candidates still need their own gate | OPEN — informative, not a prerequisite for all arithmetic work |
| X-2 | **Same-host reproduction of the new comparators** (`E0` refresh) | gufo and halo-box master / PR #91 rows are author-reported; halo-box's use `llama-bench` synthetic tokens (confound C3) | Pin builds matching each claimed row (the listed PR #91 rate pin differs from the later review pin), including gufo `fee9d2a`, halo-box HIP/Vulkan, upstream and pwilkin controls; keep Myhacsint/Halogen scope limits from the external review. Preflight known HIP gather/BF16-graph incompatibilities before long runs. Run the canonical exact-token fixture with processed-token/cache verification, serial AR and MTP separate, and BF16/F16 KV explicitly labeled. For kernel comparisons define shared dtype/layout/input/output and complete-operation boundaries before using `scripts/qwen4exp_comparator_role_map.py` (gufo mapping still needed); charge build/load/cold-capture separately | OPEN |
| X-3 | **Fresh attribution on the current default** | The family table in §1.2 predates the repair unroll, the F16 activation staging and both full-attention promotions | One role-marked `rocprofv3` capture of `code-p4096` at HEAD, 1x authority from `role-analysis.json` (§8 rule 1), reconciled to kernel interval union, copy/reset time and unprofiled request wall. Re-rank §4 from it; sample other categories/shapes when owner populations differ | CAPTURED at `2789c19d6` — current checkpoint above; additional category/shape populations remain candidate-specific |
| X-4 | **Current-default decode attribution** (was `CC-2`) | Last split is September 9 (`R6`, 54.6 ms wall / 49.3 ms device) on a composition the September 14 recovery changed | Re-run the `R6` split at HEAD before ranking any §5 row | DEVICE SPLIT CAPTURED at `2789c19d6`; stable wall and serving-wrapper attribution still OPEN under X-10/DE-5 **DEVICE SPLIT ANALYZED - THE NAMED NEXT STEP IS DONE (`20260929T`).** X-4's status read "DEVICE SPLIT CAPTURED ... stable wall and serving-wrapper attribution still OPEN", and the capture script's own `decision.next` was **"analyze marker-scoped kernel/API/role owners"**. **That analysis had never been run, and six `DE-*` rows are parked "after X-4" waiting for a device split to rank against.** Now produced: one `code-p4096` decode transition at live count 4097, snapshot-restored before each of 3 repetitions, wrapped in `rocprofv3 --runtime-trace --kernel-trace --marker-trace --kernel-rename`, then attributed to semantic roles by the established join (step windows -> innermost host-thread role range per HIP launch -> kernel by `correlation_id`). **DECODE KERNEL TIME IS 54.218 ms/step across 1,771 kernels/step and 740 roles, 100% attributed.** **Headline: `moe:expert_gate` is 24.550 ms/step = 45.28% of decode kernel time** - more than every other family combined below it, and it is a per-expert GEMM family spread over 48 layers, not one large kernel (largest single role `moe:layers.2.expert_gate` 0.724 ms/step, 1.33%; the rest cluster at 0.5-0.6, so no layer dominates). Next: `linear:attn_qkv` 4.658 (8.59%), **`linear:root.lm_head` 2.980 (5.50%) - the second largest SINGLE role is one output projection**, `linear:attn_gate` 2.890, `linear:ssm_out` 2.842, `qsa_decode:attn_q` 2.621, `gdn:attn_qkv` 2.401, `linear:attn_q` 1.815, `linear:hc_ffn_down` 1.771, `linear:hc_attn_down` 1.768. **DECODE IS NOT KERNEL-BOUND, which is the number the `DE-5`/`DE-6` runtime rows were missing**: the unprofiled wall for the same transition is median **153.0 ms** (mean 162.7, spread **1.30x**, `token_exact` and `state_exact` both true), so against 54.2 ms of kernel time that is **~35% kernel and ~65% host/launch overhead**, with 48 graph launches carrying 1,771 kernels. **The runtime side is larger than the device side for a decode step.** **REUSABLE METHOD FINDING - IN A GRAPH-REPLAYING ENGINE, ROLE ATTRIBUTION MUST NOT RELY ON THE CORRELATION-ID JOIN ALONE.** The join reached 3,516 of 5,313 kernels and missed 1,797, and **0 of the 1,797 unattributed correlation ids appear in the HIP launch trace at all** because this engine replays them from captured HIP graphs (192 `hipGraphLaunch`); a graph-replayed kernel has no launch API row to join. The fix is **not a heuristic**: `rocprofv3 --kernel-rename` renames each kernel with its enclosing roctx region, so those kernels arrive already named `qwen4exp_role:moe:layers.N.expert_gate`, and reading the role back out recovers an identity **the profiler assigned** - taking attribution to **5,313 of 5,313**. **Still OPEN**: the **stable wall** and serving-wrapper attribution remain open under X-10/DE-5 - this capture's unprofiled spread is 1.30x and the profiled arm's is **4.12x** (58.9-242.7 ms), so the ~35% kernel share is approximate and the profiled wall is not usable as a performance number. **Limits**: profiled capture, per-kernel times diagnostic; one case (`code-p4096`), one live count (4097), one model, one host, one profile, 3 repetitions; the wall is the measured transition only (snapshot restore happens before each); attribution is only as good as the roctx coverage, though there are zero unattributed kernels here. **No performance claim, no promotion, no default changed.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-x4-decode-split/README.md`, `scripts/qwen4exp_x4_decode_role_attribution.py` |
| X-5 | **Scratch-throttle audit** | halo-box PR #87: after a kernel with a private segment runs (`flash_attn_ext_f16<256>`, 252 B/lane), the command processor throttled every later scratch-enabled dispatch ~3.3x; their Q5_1 expert-down GEMV went 220 → 67 µs by replacing `volatile` float rounding barriers with a register-only `asm` barrier, bit-identical | List `private_segment_fixed_size` for every Qwen4Exp kernel in the prefill and decode census. Any non-zero scratch on a hot kernel warrants disassembly and ordered warm-process A/B: test the suspected trigger followed by the same victim, compare against a fresh process, and verify clocks and scratch allocation. A register-only barrier is a candidate only where it preserves the rounding boundary; prove parity rather than infer it from the source idiom | AUDIT COMPLETE — precondition absent tree-wide; benefit and parity unproven **AUDIT COMPLETE - THE PRECONDITION IS ABSENT TREE-WIDE (`20260929T`).** X-5's next step was "List `private_segment_fixed_size` for every Qwen4Exp kernel in the prefill and decode census. Any non-zero scratch on a hot kernel warrants disassembly and ordered warm-process A/B". **Audited: 2,245 kernels across ALL 101 `.hip` sources in the tree (101 compiled, 0 failed), and ZERO have a non-zero `private_segment_fixed_size`.** Also zero have a non-zero `group_segment_fixed_size` (static LDS - consistent with the attention family using `extern __shared__`). HEAD's teacher hash equals the value after change 2, so **exactly these two changes** lie in the range and there is no third. **Neither is a kernel swap:** `c66db0264` is a one-line profile change moving `Q4_DP4A64` into `PRODUCTION_Q8_QSA_RESTORED_FLAGS` (its own message records "0 dp4a dispatches in the baseline trace, 210 in the candidate"), and `6c3c46b04` changes the pack8 GEMV's block width at `out <= 640` as a runtime dispatch parameter. Detail: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-prefill-scope-discrepancy/README.md` **CONFIRMED on an isolated case: the `wmma` gate does not measure a prefill route.** A single canonical case (`code-p512`) at one decode step, so neither row is averaged: the post-prefill row reports **5.8014e-12** and the single decode row **1.0184e-04**, with candidate and teacher hashes differing. The prediction was made before the run - if the arms differ in decode-path selectors rather than in the prefill route, the prefill row must be flat and the decode row must not be - and it holds. So the quantity that moves is reached through `use_wmma` (`f_rowtile`, the rows-2-8 batch-decode session, `use_rowtile`), not through `wmma_prefill_f32_f32_out` as the recorded `candidate_chain` and surface claim. **The 2026-09-16 certification and its bit-identical HEAD re-run therefore measure a decode-path side effect, not the WMMA prefill route.** Open and unexplained: the heldout run shows the opposite pattern (prefill 2.06179e-03 vs decode 1.99214e-04), which no decode-path effect can produce, so something engages on the heldout prefill that does not on the canonical one. Candidate explanation, **unverified**: dispatch order - `_dispatch_can_use_wmma_prefill` requires the variant to start with `prefill_` and the exact leaf `coltile8_rowbatch4_f32_f32_out` fails that at every row count, so the route engages only if a `prefill_*` variant is carried at that point. Testable by recording the variant the dispatch carries at `_wmma_prefill_dispatch` for both protocols, which neither run does. Detail: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-prefill-scope-discrepancy/README.md` **Engagement now MEASURED at the dispatch, and it falsifies my dispatch-order explanation while sharpening the finding.** A recorder on `_wmma_prefill_dispatch` records the incoming variant, the outgoing variant and the decline reason. Canonical `code-p512`: candidate **264 engaged launches**, variant `wmma_prefill_f32_f32_out`, rows 512; heldout `code_merge_intervals`: **328 engaged**, rows 40; teacher `engaged = 0` in both with `use_wmma_false` throughout. So the route **does** engage in both protocols - my earlier prediction that the canonical prefill would never engage is **falsified** - and the post-prefill row is **still bit-identical (5.8014e-12)** while the decode row moves (1.0184e-04). `wmma_prefill_f32_f32_out` therefore produces arithmetically equivalent results to the exact chain at the prefill position: **the route runs and contributes no measurable numerical difference**, so the drift the `wmma` gate reports is not its own - it belongs to the other `use_wmma`-gated selectors (`f_rowtile`, `enabled=not use_wmma`, `use_rowtile`, the rows-2-8 batch-decode session). **A flaw in my own check corrected before it misled anyone:** the summariser reported `arms_isolated`, which was `True` here while the arms are demonstrably not isolated; renamed `wmma_route_engaged_candidate_only` with an explicit `covers_other_use_wmma_selectors: False` and a verdict that says so in words. Still open: attribution *within* those other selectors - the recorder covers the WMMA route only. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-wmma-engagement/README.md` **Attribution is now POSITIVE and measured on real weights.** Extending the recorder to the other `use_wmma`-gated selectors gives `q4k_rowtile` teacher **1.0** -> candidate **0.775128**, `wmma_prefill` 0.0 -> 0.264, `native_batch_decode_session` 0.0 -> 0.0 (ruled out); `teacher_route_engaged=False`, `selectors_observed_in_one_arm_only` empty. The 0.775128 means the candidate leaves the rowtile **inactive for 792 launches** where the teacher had it active - decode-path rows the WMMA route refuses - and since the canonical prefill row is bit-identical (5.8014e-12) while the decode row moves (1.0184e-04), **the gate's drift is the rowtile's, reached through a flag named for the route**. KL unchanged by this analysis, so the fix moved attribution not measurement. **Two bugs in my own summariser, found by reading the artifact rather than the verdict:** `teacher_route_engaged` was derived from total engagement across every selector, making it unconditionally true because `q4k_rowtile` is always active in the teacher arm (verdict read "teacher also engaged" while that arm's route count was exactly zero); and the differs-comparison collapsed each arm to a boolean, discarding the very difference it was hunting. Both fixed, rates compared instead of counts, 2 tests added. **Not** established: that the rowtile difference is harmful - 1.0184e-04 KL is inside the broad smoke floor but has not been through the production profile gates. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-wmma-engagement/README.md` **THE HELDOUT BLOCKER IS HELD OPEN FOR THE RIGHT REASON, AND THE MISSING INPUT IS NAMED (`20260930T`).** The suite cannot engage the route because its longest prompt is 71 tokens against `dense_wide`'s 257-row floor. I checked that this is a genuine row floor and not a proxy artefact before recording it: the floor is read from the dispatch guard `_q8_dense_wide_dispatch`'s `rows <= 256`, and `rows` at that guard is `len(tokens)` (`laguna_gguf_runner.py:5059`, `_execute_rows`), the real prefill token count - **not** the scratch row count, which `allocate_scratch` sizes to `min(chunk_size, max(768, prompt_tokens+1))` and which is what makes chunk 1024 and 4096 identical at p512. So a 71-token prompt really does reach the guard as 71 rows and really does decline. **The missing input is a long-form heldout source, and one exists:** `benchmarks/prompts/qwen4exp-natural-long-tasks-v1.jsonl` carries four natural long tasks, one per category, all above the floor at 1.65x-1.99x it - `long_code_booking_merge` 438 tokens, `long_general_en_release_decision` 508, `long_general_ja_retention_review` 424, `long_mixed_ja_en_acceptance` 512 (measured through the retained `build_chat_prompt` builder, not estimated). It is disjoint from the canonical 12-case fixture and is already consumed as a longform gate suite by the `HC-1` GR staged gate. **Blast radius, which is why this is not an in-place edit:** the admission suite is shared - `scripts/build_qwen4exp_admission_suite_fixture.py` records that the same 18 prompts are what `scripts/qwen4exp_layer2_profile_gate.py` and the 2026-08-16 threshold calibration consume, and the gate hardcodes both `ADMISSION_FIXTURE_SHA256` and the expected case-id set. Extending it in place would change a shared fixture and invalidate every artifact citing that hash, so the long-form protocol belongs in a sibling fixture with its own coverage record, which the gate's `category_heldout` structure already anticipates. **THE PROTOCOL WAS BUILT AND RUN, AND ITS FIRST BLOCKER IS NOW DROPPED (`20260930T`).** `scripts/build_qwen4exp_longform_heldout_fixture.py` emits a sibling fixture (`benchmarks/fixtures/qwen4exp_longform_heldout_4prompt.json`, sha256 `b2d37b8f...`) in the same per-case schema, and the gate's two heldout protocols are now one `HELDOUT_PROTOCOLS` table keyed by fixture hash carrying each protocol's kind, name and expected case-id set together, so neither can be read as the other. **The first run that can engage the promoted route is in, and `category-heldout numerical evidence` is dropped from `qualification_blockers`** (`category_heldout_evidence_complete: true`, all seven checks pass, `longest_prompt_rows` 512 against the 257-row floor, `blocked_reason: null`). Engagement was checked before any KL, as this packet family requires: the logits hashes differ, and independently the candidate ran the `dense_wide` selector 1056 times against the teacher's 0. **The route does not pass**, and it fails on exactly the scope WMMA failed on: `shape=prefill_last` and `transition=prefill_to_c1`, both 4 rows, mean KL **1.438974e-3 against a 1e-3 limit** (1.44x over) with **perfect top-1 at that scope**. The aggregate passes every metric (mean 4.33572e-4, p99 5.19085e-3, max 6.40050e-3, top-1 1.0, 3-run deterministic), and all four categories pass - so the per-scope gate is the only thing that localizes this, which is why a passing aggregate is not admissible as clearance. **The locus is the transition, not the route:** WMMA at the same window on the same host fails the same two scopes at 2.06179e-3 with top-1 1.0 there as well. Two routes failing identically at one window on KL-only with perfect top-1 points at 16-47's prefill->c1 handling rather than the `dense_wide` rewrite, and `dense_wide` is the milder of the two at that scope. The routes are **not** equivalent in aggregate - WMMA's max KL is 4.2x worse (2.70641e-2 vs 6.40050e-3) and its top-1 is 0.99556 against 1.0 - they agree only on where the per-scope failure is. **Measured on `gfx1151`, not the default W7900**, though the WMMA comparison run is same-host so that comparison is like for like. `measurement_valid: false` is expected for a heldout run and is not a defect: it is bound to the canonical protocol's flag, and this run used the heldout fixture. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-x7-longform-heldout/README.md` **THE `prefill_to_c1` SCOPE NOW HAS A MEASURED MECHANISM, AND IT IS NOT A TRANSITION (`20260930T`).** This row owns the scope. `DE-7` is decode attention/GDN *cost* (ms/token) and owns nothing here; the ledger mentioned `prefill_to_c1` only in this cell before today, so a future reader should extend this row rather than open a new one. The long-form failure is **one scope reported under two dimension names**, not two independent failures: `shape=prefill_last` and `transition=prefill_to_c1` carry identical rows/mean/p95/max (4 / 1.4390e-03 / 3.677e-03 / 4.181e-03), because `_trajectory_rows`' `trajectory[0]` is `runner.prefill(..., capture_logits=True)`'s own last-token logits - the prefill's output, not a transition step. **The recorded open question in `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-prefill-scope-discrepancy/` is now answered.** That packet found the inversion for `wmma` on the admission suite (prefill row 2.06e-03 vs decode rows 1.99e-04, against canonical 5.80e-12 / 1.02e-04), falsified its own dispatch-order explanation with the engagement recorder, and left *"why the heldout prefill row moves"* open. `dense_wide` on the long-form suite reproduces the inversion, which rules out route and prompt length as the discriminator: two 512-token prompts, one route, one process give prefill-row KL **1.3277e-12** (canonical `code-p512`) and **1.0885e-03** (longform `long_mixed_ja_en_acceptance`). **The mechanism is that the route's per-logit perturbation is the same in every protocol and only its projection onto the distribution differs:** max abs logit delta 4.0587e-01 / 4.4500e-01 / 6.2800e-01 for those two subjects plus `long_general_ja_retention_review` at 1.5955e-04 KL. KL is distributional; the deltas land where the softmax is flat on the canonical prompts and near the argmax on the long-form prompts. **Both prior readings are therefore wrong:** the canonical row's flatness is not evidence the route contributes nothing to the prefill (its logits move by 0.4), and the long-form failure is not a transition defect - there is no transition mechanism in the row. **Nondeterminism is excluded, not assumed:** every same-arm repeat in every arm and subject is bit-identical, and the long-form artifact's own `repeat_determinism` passes 3/3. **Why the scope exceeds 1e-3 is the gate working:** the scope is 4 rows with no averaging to absorb one sensitive prompt, against 1536 rows for the same limit in the canonical protocol, which is why the long-form protocol exists. **Not decided here:** whether a route with a ~0.4 per-logit delta can meet a 1e-3 mean-KL limit on a 4-row scope at every prompt - that is a scope-and-threshold question for the qualification packet, and this row stays open on it. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-prefill-last-row-probe/README.md`. **Probe limits:** `dense_wide` declines at `rows <= 256`, so the two admission subjects (40 and 55 tokens) are a negative control that measures nothing and are reported as such rather than as zeros about arithmetic.  |

### 3.1 Common decision gates

The task collector now records input-token counts and candidate host-call
observations per prompt, repeat and phase (`prefill`, `first_decode`,
`later_decode`). Arm-wide calls no longer hide which cases were inactive.
Observations are separate from output determinism comparisons; uncounted
candidates report unknown rather than false engagement. These are Python-call
counts, not GPU execution certificates (graph replay may bypass them), and
lack role/layer attribution. Long natural tasks and route-resolved GPU evidence
are still required by X-11; this instrumentation alone qualifies no candidate.
Supplemental natural-task JSONL files can be added with `--extra-prompts`;
canonical suites are always loaded, and `canonical_suite_complete` is separate
from completeness of the combined selection. Reports hash all suite files.
`--prompt-token-budget` reserves input capacity in addition to the output cap
and rejects oversized chat-formatted inputs before prefill. The default remains
1024 input tokens with chunk size 1024; a larger budget is not evidence that a
candidate ran or that a new shape passed its numerical gate.
The supplemental [natural long tasks](../benchmarks/prompts/qwen4exp-natural-long-tasks-v1.md)
cover all four categories with predeclared review criteria. Their measured
chat-formatted lengths are 438/508/424/512 tokens on the target tokenizer;
lengths alone establish neither active dispatch nor task quality.
The [dense-Q8 supplemental screen](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-natural-long-dense-q8/README.md)
observes 398 candidate host calls per prefill on every task/repeat, with
repeatable outputs and zero tracked live allocations after close. Task
qualification does not pass: both arms truncate code at 2048 tokens and give
contradictory backup-retention conclusions. Shared failures do not isolate
candidate arithmetic regression; GPU role/layer attribution remains open.
A [code-only higher-cap run](../benchmarks/results/2026-09-28-qwen4exp-natural-long-code-eos/README.md)
reaches EOS at 2424 tokens with identical arm/repeat outputs and unchanged
2048-token prefixes. The reviewed implementation passes 11 generated tests
and six independent sandboxed cases. This resolves truncation, not the
language-task failures or full qualification; output fences and weak generated
revision-test coverage are also documented.

Implementation classes are T0 (reference-preserving), T1 (local arithmetic
drift), T2 (association/layout drift), and T3 (representation or algorithm
change), as defined in EXECUTION-PROFILES. Track labels describe the proposed
approach, not a qualification verdict.

Rows marked A aim to preserve arithmetic; prove that claim on real shapes and
state boundaries. A non-bit-identical result may instead be declared T1/T2 and
reviewed under the unchanged production envelope. It is not automatically a
rejection. Every production candidate needs a registered strict fallback,
strict-teacher category/heldout mean and tail KL/top-1, deterministic repeats,
exact control/ownership, same-width isolation, applicable BF16-relative and task
gates, plus complete-owner and complete-request timing. State values may drift;
positions, masks, destinations, accepted-prefix accounting and lifecycle may not.

Memory/runtime experiments additionally need admission and cancellation/reuse
coverage. Stale performance hints may not select nondeterministic arithmetic or
supply authoritative route/position metadata; scratch must cover worst-case
populations. Representation changes (weight/KV formats or selection/acceptance
policy) are explicit separate configurations, not silent T1/T2 promotions. Do
not change the numerical envelope to match a headline. `X-7` closes missing
baseline evidence; the separate recalibration proposal does not amend policy.

## 4. Prefill open work, by functional block

Rank measured candidates inside each block by recoverable complete-request
milliseconds; newly added unmeasured rows are not a numerical ranking. Across
blocks the order is: **B routed experts, A dense, D hyper-connections,
H chunk size, E attention, F GDN, C router/reduce**, pending `X-3`.

### 4A. Dense projections — 5234.8 ms at the anchor (31.3%)

Wide route 1404.2 ms at layers 16-47; exact coltile chain 3262.5 ms at 0-15;
565 ms of other dense roles. `dense_wide256` runs the packet shape (rows 1024,
K 2560, N 10240) in 2.573 ms, **20.9 TFLOP/s**; gufo reports 30-34 TFLOP/s for
its F16 dense kernels at 2048 rows and rejected hipBLASLt at 19-26.

Then screen a predeclared selective role set or 13-47/14-47/15-47, validate on disjoint heldouts, and gate the whole composition; do not spend a summed KL budget. `X-1` is complementary | B | **CLOSED as a scope change** (`20260917T053701`). The wide route is a retiling of the f16 WMMA arithmetic, not a reassociation, so it inherits that arm's drift: 16-47 passes (mean KL 3.80e-4), 12-47 fails on `general_en` (6.47e-4), 8-47 fails on max KL (7.30e-2) and top-1 (0.98966). Drift grows monotonically with the number of f16 layers, and the promoted scope already spends ~40% of `general_en`'s headroom, so at most a few extension layers remain for at most ~0.4 s. **Reopening needs an arithmetic change, not a scope change.** The matrix cores take only f16/bf16 operands, so the wide tile's operand precision cannot be improved in place; the remaining route is an LDS-staged f32 FMA tile that keeps exact arithmetic and takes the wide tile's weight-traffic advantage. `DP-8` has since taken the exact chain's scheduling headroom down by 6.84% (`20260929T083913`). `R04`, `R07` | **The stated reopening route was tried, and it does not reopen 0-15.** `staged_f32_bm128_bn64_bk32` is that LDS-staged f32 FMA tile - it keeps the staging and tiling and multiplies in f32 on f32 operands, so the only arithmetic difference from the exact chain is the summation order. Measured at 0-15 on the canonical protocol it **fails three independent criteria**: mean KL 1.17743e-3 against the 1e-3 limit, p95 6.12058e-3 against 5e-3, top-1 0.986434 against 0.99, with 7 rows over review (0-11 is worse: 1.274e-3, 10 rows). The same mechanism **passes** at 16-47 (3.236e-4, top-1 0.99483), so the limit is which layers are converted, not the tile. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-dp5-consumer-census/README.md` **CAST BANDWIDTH MEASURED, AND THE WHOLE-ROW ESTIMATE IS REFUTED (`20260930T`).** The 4/6 model assumed both kernels run at the same effective rate; measured against the raw trace filtered to the `qwen4exp_prefill_p4096_` window (elements = `Grid_Size_X*Y*Z` as **work-items** per §8 rule 2, reproducing the artifact's 1,056 launches / 131.001 ms exactly), **it does not hold uniformly - apparent bandwidth RISES as the transfer shrinks**: **192.1 GB/s at 10,485,760 elements/launch (the `hc_*_down` pair, 256 launches, 16.106 GB, 83.861 ms)**, 241.1 at 6,291,456, 325.9 at 2,621,440, and **595.4 at 655,360**. The total, **229.0 GB/s, is 103.6% of the 221 GB/s DRAM roof** - impossible for pure DRAM traffic - so the small roles are partly **L2-served** and the 6 B/element model is not a DRAM model for them. **Therefore: the defensible target is the HC down pair alone, 16.106 GB at 192.1 GB/s = 86.9% of roof, net 4/6 x 83.861 = 55.9 ms = 0.36% of the union; and the whole-row 87.3 ms figure is REFUTED as an over-estimate** because it applies a DRAM byte model to roles whose traffic is partly cache-served and would not be saved. **This strengthens the epilogue-first order for a second independent reason**: the HC pair is not only 64.0% of the cast by time, it is the only part whose traffic is actually DRAM-bound. **Method warning, recorded because it bit this analysis**: the first attempt joined `captures.child.launch_census` shapes to `audit.prefill` times across two different captures and produced plausible per-role numbers; the 103.6%-of-roof total is what caught it. Cross-capture shape/time joins must be checked against a physical bound before use. **Not yet built.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-dp5-consumer-census/README.md` **THE BUILD IS RE-SCOPED: IT IS A RUNTIME BUFFER-OWNERSHIP CHANGE, NOT AN EPILOGUE EDIT (`20260930T`).** The row reads as a bounded kernel change - "move the F16 write into the HC mix epilogue first" - so the code was checked before starting it, and **the change cannot be made as described**. The cast is `_launch_dense_wide_f16_staged` (`hipengine/runtime/gguf_linear.py:644`), which **unconditionally** stages its input: `_wide_f16_activation_stage_ptr(count)` (line 634) returns a single `workspace.ptr` when `nbytes >= count*2`, the code casts `x_ptr -> stage_ptr`, then launches the F16 sibling. **There is no "input already F16" check on that path, and none is possible with the current ownership**: `wide_f16_activation_session` (line 568) binds **exactly one** `WideF16ActivationWorkspace(ptr, nbytes)` as a ContextVar - **one bounded scratch buffer for the whole route, shared by every consumer and overwritten on every launch**. So there is **no place for a producer to publish a persistent F16 copy and no protocol for a consumer to accept one**; DP-5 needs a new per-activation F16 buffer plus a publish/accept contract between the producing and consuming ops. **That is a runtime ownership change, not a kernel epilogue edit**, and it must be weighed against the measured prize: **55.9 ms = 0.36% of prefill** (the HC down pair only, at 86.9% of the DRAM roof). **What stands and is worth keeping**: the consumer census (HC down pair = 64.0% of the cast), the measured 192.1 GB/s, the scope (layers 16-47 only), and that the HC pair is the only DRAM-bound part - so if the contract is ever built, that is where to apply it. **Not built; no kernel changed; no default changed.** Evidence: `hipengine/runtime/gguf_linear.py:568,634,644`; `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-dp5-consumer-census/README.md`

The less-examined part is the other kernel pair's `grid=163840`/`grid=40960` `expert_gate` shapes. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-ledger-completion-audit/README.md`.  **CLOSED - THE TARGET WAS ALREADY CAPTURED BY A SIBLING ROW'S PROMOTION (`20260930T`).** This row was re-ranked UP on the strength of a capture in which `gguf_q4_k_selected_dual_silu_logical128_t64` is the largest less-examined decode owner at 13.968 ms/step. That capture PREDATES the `DE-1` promotion of the decode dp4a logical128 route, and the owner IS `DE-1`'s kernel: after the promotion it runs **5.404 ms/step at 164.48 GB/s = 89.1% of the in-block comparator** (`dense_gemv_out_kernel` fp32 router GEMV, 184.66 GB/s, same step), a gap that fell from 2.94x to 1.12x and left **0.591 ms/step = 1.25% of the 47.390 ms/step decode kernel sum**. The screen therefore answers itself: the owner is NEAR ROOF, not latency-bound, and there is no surviving shape worth an ordered-load-ahead or a preselected library algorithm. **The traffic model was re-derived independently from the model's own GGUF metadata** rather than taken from the row: `embedding_length 2560`, `expert_feed_forward_length 640`, `expert_count 512`, `expert_used_count 10`, `block_count 48`, Q4_K at `QK_K 256` / 144 B per block gives `48 x 10 x 640 x 1440 x 2 = 884.74 MB/step`, which sits 0.5% below the 888.83 MB/step the recorded rates were computed from (the difference is a shared-expert term this unique-expert model omits, so the derived value is a strict floor). Both recorded rates reproduce: 884.74 MB / 14.013 ms = 63.18 GB/s and 884.74 MB / 5.404 ms = 163.72 GB/s. **A note on how this row's own `grid=40960` reading should be treated:** it came from the launch-signature inference the row already flagged as non-unique (`--kernel-rename` replaces decode kernel names by construction), and the authoritative per-kernel join charges 10 unique experts per layer, not a 64-row expansion. The signature figure is superseded; the join is the basis. **What is NOT closed and does not belong to this row:** the decode DOWN leg `q5_1_selected_weighted_sum_logical256_t64` still runs at 113.06 GB/s = 51.2% of roof (5.444 ms/step), but it is `DE-1`'s family, and that row has already measured its obvious structure - the plain warp256 down kernel is T0 bit-exact and **13.7% slower**, while the 5.8x belongs to the non-exact `fast` variant rejected on numerics. **Pinned by** `tests/test_unit_qwen4exp_dp9_decode_moe_closure.py`, whose five assertions were each verified RED by mutation (wrong top-k, the pre-promotion owner time, removal from either side of the restoration mechanism). **Limits:** attribution from existing artifacts, not a new run; one model, one quant, one live count, one host; the rates inherit the source capture's own caveat that profiled per-kernel times are diagnostic, which would narrow but cannot close an 89.1%-of-comparator result. |

Using the engine's built-in opt-in readback (`HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_RISK_DIAGNOSTICS`, consumed by `qwen4exp_profile_gap.py --risk-diagnostics`), one production-profile `code-p4096` prefill on the usual host: **94 calls, 4,928,307,200 outputs, 35,083,610 risky, AGGREGATE REPAIR RATE 0.7119%** (max per-call 0.7797%, **over-capacity calls 0** - the risk queue never overflowed). Derived: 52,428,800 outputs and **373,230 risky per call**; 40,960 compact rows per call; each repair re-reads a K=2560 Q4_K row = **1,440 B**, so **537.5 MB per call and 50.5 GB over all calls**, giving an **effective scattered bandwidth of 40.9 GB/s** at **35.2 ns per repair**. **40.9 GB/s is far below this part's memory peak and, combined with EG-3's finding that moving 3.3x fewer bytes made the kernel SLOWER, rules out a bandwidth limit and confirms the latency-bound dependent chain.** **The prize, quantified (which EG-3 did not state)**: q4_k repair 573.211 ms against a 2,713.953 ms fast path at a 0.7119% rate - ideal repair at fast-path efficiency would be 19.32 ms, a **29.7x disproportion**, saving **553.9 ms = 2.44% of the window**; the q5_1 route (661.210 ms repair) has an **UNMEASURED rate** and its 2.87% is an explicitly-labelled inference, so the **5.31% combined ceiling assumes both routes behave identically** and the defensible measured claim is **2.44%**. The repair does **0.71% of the work in 22.7% of the MoE GEMM time**. **MX-1 comparison**: MX-1 removes the repair entirely (up to 5.44%) but changes the arithmetic and needs the full production numerical gate; a repair-efficiency fix would keep the arithmetic and need no such gate, but EG-3's evidence says no known mechanism achieves it. One prompt, one model, one host, one profile, one repetition; the q5_1 rate is unmeasured; the "ideal repair" figure is a **bound, not a proposal**; the 50.5 GB assumes K=2560 for both halves. Diagnostic only, no default changed. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-repair-rate/README.md` |

(An earlier ledger revision called that "above this host's ~9.5 TFLOP/s FP32 peak"; the measured FP32 ceiling in `20260915T182229` is ~28.5 TFLOP/s with dual issue, so that figure alone does not prove tensor-core use. Corrected, the actual QSA kernels total **8.418 ms of 3914.468 ms = 0.22%** (`qsa_split_norm_rope_rows` 0.14%, `qsa_gate_context` 0.06%, `qsa_norm_rope_rows` 0.01%, `qsa_scatter_index_keys` 0.00%). Full attention is separately **1.27%**, so **every attention-arithmetic candidate in this ledger (QA-2, QA-4, QA-6, QA-7) shares a combined ceiling of ~1.5% of prefill** - while MoE (47.39%) and GR (34.18%) are 81.6%. The diagnosis above is untouched and the direction may still be right; this row is now **correctly cost-gated as low-yield**, and a Tier-B tensor-core build should not be started ahead of the MoE/GR families. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-family-share/README.md`. **DIAGNOSIS CORRECTED, DIRECTION STRENGTHENED (`20260929T`).** The cited evidence - "HEADS 2 -> 4 halves K/V traffic and buys only 1.08x", read as issue-limited-not-traffic-limited - is **confounded**: that experiment changed three things at once. Census (`scripts/qwen4exp_repair_register_census.py --source .../qwen4_exp_qsa.hip`; `template <bool PAGE256, int HEADS=1>`, `__launch_bounds__(32)` = one wave32/block): **HEADS=1 40 VGPR / 262 ins / 32 waves/CU / 100.0%; HEADS=2 66 / 331 / 28 / 87.5%; HEADS=4 108 / 442 / 18 / 56.2%**. So HEADS 2 -> 4 **halved traffic (helps) while cutting occupancy 87.5% -> 56.2% and raising instructions +33.5% (both hurt)** - a net 1.08x from three opposing changes cannot isolate an issue-limit, and the near-cancellation is sufficient explanation on its own. **The occupancy loss is INHERENT, not an allocation choice**: forcing `__launch_bounds__(32, N)` for N=16/32/40/64 leaves HEADS=4 at 96-108 VGPRs with **zero spills**, and the compiler reports `-Wpass-failed`: "desired occupancy was 64, final occupancy is **12**" for HEADS=4 (vs 16 for HEADS=1/2). At a 32-VGPR demand - a 3.4x reduction - it still emits 108 and spills nothing, so the live range genuinely requires them. **Corrected mechanism: register-file CAPACITY, not issue width.** Widening the scalar tile buys traffic but pays occupancy and the two nearly cancel - which is why the scalar route has no cheap win. **This strengthens the tensor-core direction for a more specific reason than the row states**: the register cost comes from holding the wide tile and its intermediates in VGPRs, and the matrix units do that arithmetic without keeping those intermediates live, so tensor cores are the route that takes the traffic saving WITHOUT paying the occupancy that defeated the scalar wide tile. **No candidate implemented** - the Tier-B build remains the experiment; this packet corrects its mechanism and gives it a measured basis. ### 4F. GDN — 821.6 ms (4.9%)

| ID | Mechanism / measured cost | Opportunity and limit | First bounded experiment | Track | State |

| GD-3 | **Latency-pipelined token windows.** gufo's F32 row-split recurrence stages four tokens at a time through LDS and keeps three windows of loads in flight because each step otherwise pays a DRAM latency; theirs is ~65 ms per 1K tokens against our 205 | F32 arithmetic; a different reduction layout, so exactness against our parent must be checked, not assumed | Measure stall cycles in our register-state kernel first; if latency-bound, add the windowed prefetch while keeping our per-row reduction order | A | CLOSED — STALE PREMISE, AND THE LEVER IS BOUNDED AT ~1% OF PREFILL **MEASURED (`20260930T`): THE ROW'S BASELINE DOES NOT EXIST AND ITS COMPARISON IS INVERTED.** The row asserts we are 3.15x SLOWER than gufo ("theirs is ~65 ms per 1K tokens against our 205") and asks to "measure stall cycles in our register-state kernel first; if latency-bound, add the windowed prefetch". All four registered recurrence routes were measured directly on gfx1100 at a realistic shape (Hk16/Hv48/Dk=Dv=128), 5 reps after 1 warmup, per 1K tokens: **`tiled16` (the production route) 4.961 ms**, `register_state` 5.687, `columnwarps` 7.610, `serial_lds` 39.628. **The row's "our 205" matches NO route** - the worst registered route is 5.2x faster than it and the production route is **41x** faster - and **gufo's quoted 65 ms is 13x SLOWER than our production route**, so the row's direction is inverted: we are ahead, not behind. Scaling is exactly linear in tokens (2.898/5.665/11.278 ms at 512/1024/2048), so there is no fixed overhead. **The MECHANISM is confirmed:** every route sits at **1-9% of the 221 GB/s bandwidth roof** and 0.1-1.1% of the FP32 FLOP roof (traffic floor 0.448 ms, FLOP floor 0.053 ms at 1K), so none is traffic-bound and latency/occupancy/issue is the only remaining explanation - supported structurally by the register-state kernel holding **128 floats of state per thread** and carrying roughly **16 `__syncthreads()` per token**, against a column-warp sibling explicitly built with "no `__syncthreads` in the token loop". **THE COST GATE IS AN UNCONDITIONAL UPPER BOUND:** one layer's recurrence is 4.961 ms/1K, there are **9 certified GDN layers**, so ~22.7 ms at p508 against today's **2014 ms** p512 prefill = **1.1%** - meaning even an INFINITELY fast recurrence saves at most 1.1% of prefill, and a realistic 2x recovers ~0.55%. **Redirection, not a rejection of the mechanism:** the GDN family's cost is overwhelmingly OUTSIDE the recurrence (~3% of the family on the last recorded 750.68 ms trace), so if GDN is worth optimizing the norms/gates/conv/prepare/snapshot stages are where the time is - a different row with its own measurement. **Not claimed:** the family's current share of prefill (the 750.68 ms figure is from a 6.500 s prefill and has not been re-measured). One host, one shape, synthetic inputs, wall medians around a device synchronize (launch overhead included, negligible at 5-40 ms/call); no `rocprofv3` stall counters were taken, so "latency-bound" is inferred from distance to both roofs. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd3-recurrence-screen/README.md`, `scripts/qwen4exp_gd3_recurrence_screen.py` **CORRECTION (`20260930T`): THE CEILING ABOVE IS WRONG BY 4.5x - IT IS ~5% OF PREFILL, NOT 1.1%.** The error is the layer count: "9 certified GDN layers" is the **numerical qualification scope** (global layers 35-47), not the model's GDN layer count. A `rocprofv3 --kernel-trace` of the p512 prefill establishes **48 layers = 36 GDN + 12 full attention** three independent ways from the same trace - GDN recurrence dispatches **72** = 36x2 passes, router dispatches **96** = 48x2, conv dispatches **144** = 2x36x2 - and measures the GDN family directly at **194.487 ms of 3914.468 ms kernel time = 4.97%** (about two passes: 3914/2014 = 1.94). Corrected: **36 x 2.54 ms at p512 = 91.4 ms of 2014 ms = ~4.5%**, matching the measured 4.97%. **The row's DIRECTION survives** (its cited baseline still matches no registered route, and gufo's 65 ms is still ~13x slower than our production route), but its cost gate was overstated by 4.5x. **The "redirection" paragraph above is WITHDRAWN as wrong**: it claims the family's cost is "overwhelmingly OUTSIDE the recurrence", but the single kernel `qwen4_exp_gdn_prefill_f32_kernel<true, true>` accounts for ALL 194.487 ms of the family - there is no hidden 97% to redirect to. The GDN-adjacent stages are small and separately classified (conv 13.625 ms = 0.35%, norm 16.178 ms = 0.41%), so **GD-4's ceiling is the same ~5% plus those two, ~5.5%** - the more complete version of the same idea, not a separate prize. **The mechanism is now confirmed by HARDWARE evidence rather than inference**: the trace records the recurrence dispatch at **`VGPR_Count` 256 (the hardware maximum)**, `SGPR_Count` 128, `LDS_Block_Size` 0, workgroup 128, grid 6144 - at 256 x 32 x 4 B = 32 KB against a 64 KB VGPR file per SIMD that permits **2 waves per SIMD** of a 64-wave-per-CU maximum, ~12.5% occupancy, so "latency-bound" is right and the cause is specifically **register-file capacity, not issue width** (the same conclusion QA-2 reached independently for QSA). Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-family-share/README.md`, `worklog/entries/20260930T013003.667156Z-lhl-qwen4exp-prefill-family-share-d3753c.md` |
| GD-4 | **Chunked GDN on fp16 WMMA** (halo-box PR #91: chunk scan with fused conv/SiLU, q/k norm and gated RMS norm; NMSE ~2e-7 against the sequential kernel) | Changes arithmetic in the recurrence, which carries state across the whole prompt | Screen state drift over 4K prompts and chunk/tail boundaries before a full production gate; `X-1` supplies context, not admission | B | OPEN, numerical/state screen first — **COST GATE MEASURED (`20260930T`): the ceiling is ~5.5% of prefill, and it is the same lever as GD-3, not a separate one.** A `rocprofv3 --kernel-trace` of the p512 prefill puts the **GDN family at 194.487 ms of 3914.468 ms kernel time = 4.97%**, and the single recurrence kernel `qwen4_exp_gdn_prefill_f32_kernel<true, true>` accounts for **all** of it. The stages this row's reference fuses on top are separately classified and small: **conv 13.625 ms (0.35%)** and **norm 16.178 ms (0.41%)**. So the fused chunk scan's ceiling is roughly **5.5%** - real, and 5x larger than the 1.1% first attributed to GD-3 (that figure was wrong; see GD-3's correction), but **not a separate prize from GD-3 and not where prefill time is**. **For scale:** MoE is **47.39%** and GR is **34.18%** of prefill - **81.6% between them** - with four individual kernels at **67.7%**, so GDN work of any kind competes for a ~5.5% slice. Two of those four are already on their optimal known path: `gguf_k_prefill_out_coltile_rowbatch_pipelined` (18.05%) is *already* the two-stage K-pipelined form, and `gguf_q4_k_selected_dual_wmma_iu8_risk_prefill` (21.77%) is the fastest registered owner by `ED-4`. **Status: still OPEN on its own terms** (no state-drift screen has been run, and the arithmetic does change in the recurrence, which carries state across the whole prompt), but it should be prioritized below the MoE/GR families. The mechanism evidence is now hardware-grade: the recurrence runs at `VGPR_Count` 256 (the hardware maximum), i.e. ~12.5% occupancy, so the register-file-capacity argument that makes a chunked/WMMA reformulation attractive is measured rather than inferred. **STATE-DRIFT SCREEN RUN, AND THE ROW'S STATED BLOCKER IS CLEARED (`20260930T`).** The recurrence is affine in the state, so a chunked composition is *exact in exact arithmetic* and the only question is floating-point reordering. `scripts/gdn_chunk_drift_screen.py` screens it against `replay_gdn` (the same sequential reference the GPU-verification harness uses) at Qwen4Exp's shape over a 4K prompt: **fp32 state carry gives NMSE 4.6e-14 to 7.1e-14, flat in chunk size (32/64/128/256) and indistinguishable from fp32 round-off; an fp16 state carry costs ~4 orders of magnitude and is still tiny (NMSE 9.0e-11 to 8.0e-10), improving with larger chunks because fewer boundary roundings occur - and even the worst case is ~1000x better than the row's own cited reference (PR #91's "NMSE ~2e-7").** **No chunk/tail boundary effect:** 4095, 4096, 4097 and 4100 tokens all sit at NMSE ~7.2e-14. A RED test first confirmed the chunked form converges to the sequential one as the chunk grows (NMSE 4.6e-14 to 7.4e-14 with no growth in T), which is what makes the figures meaningful rather than a measurement of a different algorithm. **The screen also found the one thing a kernel author most needs, and it is not what the row expected:** computing `D_t/D_j` as a **ratio of prefix products** is numerically unsafe - `cum` decays geometrically and underflows to zero non-simultaneously, so while `cum[j]` has underflowed and `cum[i]` has not, the clamped ratio explodes, `L` blows up and the solve diverges (measured max_abs **1.05e1 at chunk 256 against 2.26e-01 at chunk 64** - larger chunks *worse*, the signature of an explosion). A **direct suffix product** `prod_{k=j+1..i} decay[k]` never divides, so every entry stays bounded by 1 and underflows monotonically; that form is committed and locked by `tests/test_unit_gdn_chunk_drift_screen.py`, which fails 5 ways when the prefix form is reinstated. **Remaining work is the kernel build, not more numerics.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-drift/README.md`. | **BUILD FEASIBILITY PRICED, AND IT JUSTIFIES THE BUILD (`20260930T`).** GD-3's screen established the recurrence is not traffic-bound but did not establish what a chunked scan would cost. From GD-3's own measured sequential rate (`tiled16`, **4.961 ms per 1K tokens per layer**) and GD-4's already-passed drift screen: the sequential route does **3 x 128x128 = 49,152 MACs per token per v-head** (memory `k.S`, state update `k (x) delta`, output `q.S`) = **4.832 GFLOP per 1K tokens per layer, achieving 974 GFLOP/s = 1.60% of the 61 TFLOP/s fp32 peak**. **The chunked scan adds only `C/384` extra FLOPs** - the L and QK `C x C` matrices, `C^2 x 128` MACs per chunk per head against the sequential route's three 128x128 products per token: **+8.3% at chunk 32, +33.3% at chunk 128, +66.7% at chunk 256**. **Break-even throughput is 1.05-1.62 TFLOP/s = 1.7-2.7% of the fp32 peak** - so the chunked schedule needs only to be about **twice** as efficient as a route sitting at 1.6% of peak, while exposing **C x more parallelism** along the token axis, which is the axis the sequential route has none of. **Chunk 128 is the recommended operating point**: +33.3% FLOPs, 1.30 TFLOP/s break-even, and fp16 state-carry NMSE **1.824e-10** - 5.4x better than chunk 32, because the drift screen found fp16 NMSE *improves* with larger chunks. **What this does NOT show**: that the chunked scan wins. It shows the arithmetic cost is small enough that a win is possible; whether the schedule reaches the 1.3 TFLOP/s bar is what the build must demonstrate. **Correction of my own earlier estimate**: an early pass in this session put the chunked scan at "16x more FLOPs" by comparing against a single 128x128 product rather than three; the true ratio is `C/384`. **Counter constraint re-confirmed**: PMC counters cannot establish the mechanism - `RDNA3-TUNING-GUIDE.md` §4.9 records that every `rocprofv3` counter collection on this stack hangs before the profiled application starts and that `timeout` cannot kill it (SIGTERM handler blocks; use `timeout -k 10`). GD-3 was right to avoid them. **Ceiling: the GDN recurrence is the whole family - 194.487 ms of 3,914.468 ms (4.97%) at p512, and the `gdn` role is 844.295 ms of the 15,741.963 ms union (5.36%) in the X-3 p4096 window - so removing it entirely is worth ~5% of the request and a 2x gain ~2.5%.** Arithmetic model and committed measurements only; **no kernel built and no chunked schedule timed**. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-scan-feasibility/README.md` **BUILD PLAN DERIVED, AND THREE OF ITS FOUR CONSTRAINTS ARE NOT IN THIS ROW (`20260930T`).** Working the design against the validated reference (`scripts/gdn_chunk_drift_screen.py::_chunk_local`) and the existing ABI surfaced constraints that decide whether the kernel can reach the priced break-even. **(1) L and QK are value-column independent** - they depend only on k, q and the decay - so every warp of a v-head needs the same shared data and it **must be computed cooperatively into shared memory**. Computing it per-warp redundantly costs **16x: +66.7% at chunk 16 and +133.3% at chunk 32, against +4.17% and +8.33% cooperative.** This is the difference between the priced **1.05 TFLOP/s break-even and a route that must reach ~15 TFLOP/s** - it converts a 2%-of-peak bar into a 25%-of-peak bar, and it is not diagnosable from timing alone. **(2) The LDS operating point**: staging kn, qn, L, ratio and delta costs 26.3 KB at chunk 16 and 56.6 KB at chunk 32 (both fit the 64 KB workgroup limit; chunk 64 is 129.2 KB and does not). **Chunk 32 is primary** - it fits and its fp16 state-carry NMSE 7.961e-10 is better than chunk 16's, because the drift screen found fp16 NMSE *improves* with larger chunks; chunk 16 is the fallback if LDS-limited occupancy binds. **(3) The block decomposition must change, and this is the structural work**: `tiled16`'s grid is `(num_v_heads=48, col_blocks=8)`, so **8 blocks per v-head would each recompute the shared L** - exactly the 8x redundancy constraint 1 forbids - while collapsing to one block per v-head gives only **48 blocks against 96 CUs, so half the device idles**. Filling the device needs chunk-axis parallelism, i.e. **a two-kernel design with a chunk-level state-propagation pass. This is not a one-kernel port of `tiled16`.** **(4) The suffix product is an in-kernel requirement, not just a reference one**: the kernel's `ratio` matrix *and* the state-carry weights `ratio[-1,:]` must be direct suffix products, or the solve diverges (measured max_abs 1.05e1 at chunk 256 vs 2.26e-01 at chunk 64 - larger chunks worse). **BUILD PLAN ONLY - no kernel written, compiled or timed.** Recorded so the build does not rediscover it. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-gd4-chunk-scan-buildplan/README.md`
