If the +0.228 s / +0.126 s effects are inside noise as the Evidence column asserts, the row should be CLOSED; if they are real, the prescribed experiment is not an experiment. Either way the row is not actionable as written. One of those two columns needs correcting before it is worked.**|

### 4G. PLE — 9.9 ms in the warm anchor

Warm page-cache PLE is not a dominant prefill owner. The embedding table is
host-mapped, not wholly GPU-resident; “resident weights” must not erase the
cold/new-row serving case. Journey `R13`–`R15` and external `E7` remain separate,
unranked work until that workload has an exposed cost.

| ID | Mechanism / evidence | First bounded experiment and deciding gate | State |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PL-1 | **Copy elision, sorted dedup and persistent reads** (`R13`, `R14`); historical cold gains came with warm regressions | Measure gather, dedup/scatter, I/O and H2D separately on declared warm and cold/new-row category inputs. Compare the existing mmap path with one changed copy/read mechanism; inventory weight pinning/residency, startup fragmentation and KV-versus-page-cache budget, and charge host RSS/page-cache pressure and first-token wall. Advance only for a declared serving scope with a complete-request benefit, not another warm sweep or unchanged direct-to-ring layout | OPEN — workload-dependent, not a warm-kernel priority |
| PL-2 | **Asynchronous PLE/UMA staging** (`R15`, `E5`, Wilkin §3) | First measure exposed copy/wait time. If material, test one bounded double-buffer path with delayed consumers, wrap, cancellation, c2, graph pointer generations, allocation-root lifetime and ordered output consumption. Include buffer bytes in admission; reject if synchronization merely moves elsewhere | COST-GATED OUT — the exposed copy/wait gate fails **COST-GATED OUT (`20260929T`).** The gate is "first measure exposed copy/wait time"; measured, and it fails. Two independent measurements, neither needing the model: `scripts/qwen4exp_ple_h2d_cost.py` times the pinned H2D at the real shapes - prefill 512x2560 fp32 = 5.00 MiB / **67.60 us** / 77.6 GB/s; decode 1x2560 fp32 = 10.00 KiB / **16.97 us** / 0.6 GB/s (latency-bound) - giving **2.240 ms per request** (128 decode + 1 prefill, 6.25 MiB). In-engine attribution via `--ple-telemetry --case-id code-p512` (fresh run, current code): gather+dequantize **0.974 s**, staging copy 0.409 ms, H2D (decode only) 11.444 ms. **Verdict: addressable = H2D 2.240 ms + staging 0.409 ms = ~2.65 ms against the 9,055.58 ms production code-p512 wall (2026-09-07 baselines) = 0.029%.** Even perfect overlap recovers 0.029%, and one decode copy is 0.027% of a 61.75 ms decode step. The dominant PLE term is **gather/dequantize at 0.974 s (~370x the whole copy cost)** - CPU work on the mmap'd table that async staging does not touch; the 2026-09-02 artifact reached the same ranking independently. **No candidate path built**, per the gate's own "if material" condition. Also found and fixed a telemetry gap: the **prefill** PLE copy did not call `record_h2d`, so telemetry understated the request H2D payload **4x** (1,310,720 = 1.25 MiB -> **6,553,600 = 6.25 MiB** = 5,242,880 prefill + 1,310,720 decode, matching the independently measured shapes exactly); the added call is guarded like the decode path's, so it is a no-op with telemetry off and production is unaffected. `performance_claim: false` - this measures the existing synchronous copy and rejects a candidate, not an alternative. **Instrumented walls are noisy and excluded from performance evidence** (gather_dequant moved 0.974 -> 1.635 s between identical telemetry runs; h2d_wall moved 11.444 -> 11.988 ms for an added 67.60 us copy) - the deterministic **bytes** are the reliable evidence. One shape, one host. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-ple-staging-cost/README.md`|

### 4H. Chunking and runtime

| ID | Mechanism / measured cost | Opportunity and limit | First bounded experiment | Track | State |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RT-1 | **Prefill chunk 2048 / 4096** (was `CC-1`). Competitors run 2048 (gufo) and 4096 (halo-box benchmarks) | Dense half measured −23.5% (325 ms) at 4096; revised MoE estimate 0.95–1.07 s and total ~1.3 s (STATUS §5), not the older additive 1.0 + 0.6 s MoE estimate. The September 15 engine A/B measured +3.94% on 4K on a composition ~32% slower. 2048/4096 explicit options and mandatory scratch accounting were qualified on the September 15 composition; optional sidecars, graphs, verification and today's F16 activation workspace still need admission evidence | Re-measure 2048 and 4096 against today's default with real per-expert populations; measure together with `DP-7` (wide-tile TFLOP/s at larger M). Re-run the gate at the new chunk (`X-6`) | A | OPEN — largest Track A lever outside the MoE rows; **the serving half is now PROMOTED (see the last segment)**. `R11` **RE-MEASURED AGAINST TODAY'S DEFAULT (`20260929T`): chunk 4096 is **-4.94%** on `code-p4096`.** Radeon 8060S gfx1151, UD-Q4_K_XL, production profile, cached build, three repetitions per arm, one process per arm: chunk 1024 **15.625 s -> 4096 14.854 s, -771.9 ms**. The separation between medians is **5.70x** the larger within-arm spread and the arms do not overlap, so the effect is established at this sample size; a 1-rep screen also covered 2048 (-4.52%) and gave 4096 -5.77%, which the median **supersedes**. **`token_id` and `logits_sha256` are identical on every arm at every repetition**, with `fell_back_to_strict=False` - chunk size changes scheduling, not arithmetic. **Cost: peak allocation 84.44 -> 89.40 GB (+4.96 GB, +5.9%)**, an admission trade. **NOT promoted:** two concrete blockers - the numerical gate must be re-run at the new chunk size (`X-6`), and `RT-3` records that short-request costs historically kept 1024 as the default, so the right form is a shape-based policy rather than a blanket flip. The September 15 +3.94% engine A/B is neither confirmed nor contradicted (different date, changed composition) and is not presented as such. One case, one host, prefill only. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-chunk-remeasure/README.md` **FIRST PROMOTION BLOCKER CLEARED (`20260929T`): the X-6 gate re-run at chunk 4096 PASSES and both arms reproduce the chunk-1024 certification BIT-IDENTICALLY.** `--route wmma --layers 16-47 --prefill-chunk-size 4096 --decode-steps 128 --repeat-runs 3`: teacher `550bb9b832bc7ba2...` and candidate `65370710c0b9c40f...` are the same hashes the chunk-1024 run recorded (`550bb9b8` / `65370710`), mean KL 3.795524e-4 against 3.79552e-4, `hard_gates_passed: true`, p95 1.762e-3, max 1.530e-2, top-1 0.99354, `repeat_deterministic: true`, `canonical_numerical_qualification_claim: true`. So the route arithmetic AND its drift are **invariant to the prefill chunk size** over 1024->4096, not merely close. **Checked against the false-pass mode first, since this gate previously returned KL exactly 0.0 with `measurement_valid: true` on a route that never ran:** engagement is recorded rather than inferred - `candidate_route_engaged: true`, `teacher_route_engaged: false`, `wmma_prefill` candidate rate 0.373585 vs teacher 0.0, **792** `wmma_prefill_f32_f32_out` launches, `selectors_observed_in_one_arm_only: []`, KL nonzero, teacher genuinely the cleared exact chain. **Does NOT clear:** the second blocker (`RT-3` short-request behaviour and the +4.96 GB allocation) and `X-7`'s remaining profile blockers, which the gate still reports and which the chunk change neither creates nor worsens. Still not promoted. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-chunk-remeasure/README.md` **SECOND BLOCKER DOES NOT REPRODUCE (`20260929T`): there is NO short-request cost.** `code-p512` at chunk 1024 vs 4096, three reps per arm: median 2.0074 vs 2.0114 s, a +0.20% difference that is **0.08x** the larger within-arm spread with the arms overlapping, and `token_id`/`logits_sha256` identical. **Peak allocation is byte-identical (83,857,109,608 B)**, not +4.96 GB. Mechanism, from the code: `allocate_scratch` sizes every owner at `rows = min(chunk_size, runner.max_sequence_length)` and the harness sets `max_sequence_length = max(768, prompt_tokens + 1)`, so `code-p512` gives `max_seq_len` 768 and both chunk sizes resolve to 768 rows - the same execution - while `code-p4096` gives 4097 and they resolve to 1024 vs 4096, which is the +4.96 GB. So the workspace is **already shape-aware** and for any prompt <= 1023 tokens the two chunk sizes are byte-for-byte identical in time and memory. **Correction to my own earlier reasoning:** I claimed a shape-based policy would have to make the *allocation* shape-aware and that this was a larger change than `RT-3` assumes - wrong, the allocation already is; I had `min(chunk, max_seq_len)` right and wrongly assumed `max_seq_len` was constant across runs. **What remains is a single memory-admission question**: can the host afford a 89.40 GB peak on long prompts, where the +4.96 GB is paid by the prompt that gets the -4.94%. `RT-3`'s short-request objection does not reproduce here. Still not promoted. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-chunk-remeasure/README.md` **QUALIFICATION TO MY OWN MEASUREMENT (`20260929T`): the "default" in this packet is the harness's, not the server's.** Everything measured above compares **1024 -> 4096**, because 1024 is the benchmark protocol's value and what the recorded attribution command passed. But the engine has more than one chunk-size default: `EngineLoopConfig.max_prefill_chunk_tokens` (`DEFAULT_MAX_PREFILL_CHUNK_TOKENS`) is **256** and governs the serving/CLI path, while `EngineLoop.__init__`'s `prefill_chunk_size = 1024` is only the *no-config fallback* - line 1907 sets `self.prefill_chunk_size = int(resolved_config.max_prefill_chunk_tokens)`, so a supplied config overrides it with 256, and the CLI's own help says "default: 256". So the serving-relevant comparison is **256 -> 4096** (16x the chunk, not 4x) and it is **UNMEASURED**. The -4.94% is therefore a valid result for 1024 -> 4096 under this harness protocol and is **not** evidence for the size of the production change; nothing here should be cited as "the server would be 4.94% faster". The X-6 gate result is unaffected in kind - route arithmetic and drift are invariant to chunk size over the range tested, which does not depend on which default is in play - but the speedup figure does. Next measurement if pursued: 256 -> 4096 on the same case and protocol. Still not promoted. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-chunk-remeasure/README.md` **SERVING-RELEVANT COMPARISON MEASURED (`20260929T`): the win against the serving default is far larger than against the protocol value.** Since 256 - not 1024 - is what the serving path defaults to (`EngineLoopConfig.max_prefill_chunk_tokens`), the governing comparison was measured: `code-p4096`, three reps per arm, chunk 256 median **23.8886 s** vs chunk 4096 **14.6882 s** = **-9.2004 s (-38.51%)** (min-to-min -38.42%). **Arms do not meaningfully overlap**: 4096's slowest rep (14.7967) beats 256's fastest (23.8427) by 38%; the naive range-overlap test trips only because the 256 arm has a 1.32x outlier (31.5102 s) extending it upward. Honest counterweight: that outlier inflates the 256 arm's stdev to 3.6037 vs 4096's 0.0524, so |median delta| is only 2.55x the larger stdev - weaker by that statistic than the 5.70x for 1024 -> 4096 - but min-to-min and worst-vs-best are unambiguous. **Numerics: the chunk size is NOT invariant here** - `token_id` identical (248068) but `logits_sha256` DIFFERS (e717076f vs 7270a972), so this is an arithmetic change relative to today's serving default, not a pure scheduling one. Not a gate regression: X-6 passes at 4096 and hashes bit-identically to the 1024 certification, so 4096 is the certified configuration. Note the route IS invariant between 1024 and 4096 (identical hashes) but NOT between 256 and 4096 - both measured, and neither generalizes to "chunk size does not affect numerics". Memory: peak 83.20 -> 89.40 GB = **+6.20 GB**; GTT is 120 GB here, leaving 30.6 GB headroom (25.5%). **Limits: ONE case in ONE category** - the 12-case 4-category suite run is the evidence that would generalize this, and until it lands the -38.51% is single-case. Prefill only; one host, and the +6.20 GB admission cost is host-specific. **No default changed.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-serving-chunk/README.md` **FULL SUITE MEASURED (`20260929T`): 12/12 cases faster at chunk 4096, all completely separated, `token_id` identical 12/12, and `logits_sha256` identical in ZERO of 12.** All 12 fixture cases (4 categories x 3 sizes), three reps per arm, both chunk sizes, 24 invocations all exit 0. Effect is monotone in size and flat across category: p512 -28.2/-28.9/-28.7/-29.7%, p1024 -35.0/-33.4/-33.5/-34.2%, p4096 -37.6/-36.5/-35.4/-37.2%; within-size spread under 1.6pp, no category an outlier. Peak delta +0.77/+1.24/+6.20 GB by size, tracking `min(chunk, max_sequence_length)`. **So the arithmetic changes in EVERY case** - this is not a scheduling change and cannot be certified by bit-identity. It IS certified by the canonical numerical gate at chunk 4096 over this same 12-case fixture: `canonical_numerical_qualification_claim: true`, kl_mean 0.00038 / p95 0.00176 / p99 0.00542 / max 0.0153 against 0.001/0.005/0.02/0.05, top1 above floor. Stated precisely: that artifact records `profile_qualification_claim: false` with route-level blockers (category-heldout, strict-teacher/BF16-relative, exact control/isolation, task-quality, route engagement) that were **already open before this work** - not introduced by the chunk size and not cleared by it. **PROMOTION DECISION: NOT a blanket default flip, and the blocker is concrete.** `DEFAULT_MAX_PREFILL_CHUNK_TOKENS` is GLOBAL across every model the engine serves and the workspace is `min(chunk, max_seq_len)` for all of them, while the +6.20 GB is measured for ONE model on ONE host - so a blanket 4096 multiplies the prefill workspace everywhere and turns a working configuration into a possible OOM on a lower-memory host. Bounding facts this establishes: (1) the historical short-request objection is DEAD (a 512-token request is unaffected in time and memory - the workspace is already shape-aware), so the only remaining constraint is peak memory; (2) there is no scoped hook - this model has no geometry-keyed chunk entry (the `GGUF_PREFILL_n` pattern) and the runner has no memory-aware admission guard, so today the choice is a global flip or new mechanism. **Concrete next step if pursued: memory-bounded chunk selection** (largest chunk the host can admit, falling back toward 256) - a real change with its own test/gate burden, not a constant edit. Limits: one host, one model, prefill only, numerical-not-profile gate. **No default changed.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-serving-suite/README.md` **DECISIVE COMPARISON MEASURED (`20260929T`): 256 -> 1024, both arms in ONE run (REFACTOR forbids cross-run chunk comparisons), all 12 cases, three reps per arm, 24 artifacts all exit 0. **12/12 faster, 12/12 completely separated, `token_id` identical 12/12, `logits_sha256` identical 0/12.** p512 -29.0 to -29.4%, p1024 -32.9 to -34.4%, p4096 -32.9 to -35.7%; flat across category. **The marginal trade is the finding:** at `code-p4096`, 256 -> 1024 is **-34.85% for +1.24 GB**, while 256 -> 4096 is -37.61% for +6.20 GB - so 1024 -> 4096 buys **2.76 further percentage points for 4.96 GB more**. 256 -> 1024 captures **93% of the win at 20% of the memory**, so **1024 is the promotion candidate, not 4096**. Peak delta is flat at +1.24 GB for p1024/p4096 because the workspace is `min(chunk, max_seq_len)`. **Chunk 1024 is the CERTIFIED configuration** - the X-6 gate's numerical certification is at chunk 1024 and hashes bit-identically at 4096 - so this promotion lands on the configuration the gate actually certifies. `logits_sha256` still differs from 256 in all 12 cases, so it is an ARITHMETIC change relative to the current serving default. **PROMOTION BLOCKER - and it is NOT the memory cost, which at +1.24 GB is small:** `max_prefill_chunk_tokens` lives in `EngineLoopConfig` beside `fair_prefill_burst_chunks`, `round_prefill_token_budget` and `round_decode_row_budget`; its CLI help reads "Maximum prefill chunk tokens per loop tick" and dispatch caps it again at the round budget. It is a **C4 scheduler knob bounding per-tick prefill work so decode is not starved**, not a model-shape parameter. Raising it 4x makes each prefill tick up to 4x longer; the measured -29% to -36% is PREFILL THROUGHPUT and says nothing about DECODE LATENCY UNDER CONCURRENCY, which is what the tick bound exists to protect. That is unmeasured, and it is the prerequisite REFACTOR already names ("qualify ... c2 behavior"). So: prefill throughput for 256 -> 1024 is MEASURED; the serving default is BLOCKED on the interleaving/fairness measurement. A prefill-only or batch path is directly supported. Limits: one host, one model, prefill only, no c2/c8 claim. **No default changed.** Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-256-vs-1024/README.md` **CHUNK-INVARIANCE CLASS NARROWED (`20260929T`): the class is exactly {1024, 2048, 4096} - CHUNK 256 IS OUTSIDE IT.** Reading the published `logits_sha256` across all three RT-1 packets (no re-run): **chunk 1024 vs 4096 is identical on 12 of 12 canonical cases**, an independent three-point run on `code-p4096` confirms 1024 = 2048 = 4096 (`7270a972da43ff46...`), and **chunk 256 vs 4096 differs on 12 of 12 cases**. So this row's "chunk size changes scheduling, not arithmetic" claim is **correct within the >= 1024 class and FALSE at 256** - it should be read as scoped, not wrong, since its own arms were 1024 and 4096. **`token_id` IS invariant across all four chunk sizes including 256** (12/12), so the 256 divergence is **sub-argmax**: it does not change the chosen token on these cases but is a real arithmetic difference that could change a near tie. **Chunk 256 is not numerically interchangeable with 1024+** - any comparison mixing the two (a baseline at 256 against a candidate at 1024, or a gate run at 256 quoted for a 1024 deployment) compares two configurations, the same protocol-confound class the external review checklist C1-C6 warns about. **Likely mechanism (HYPOTHESIS, not established)**: chunk size sets the per-chunk row count and several kernels are selected by row/M thresholds (dense wide-256, coltitle/rowbatch families, staged routes); a threshold between 256 and 1024 rows would select a different kernel and hence a different reduction order. No dispatch trace was taken. **This also predicts the X-6 gate passes at chunk 4096**: the certification is at 1024 and 1024 vs 4096 is bit-identical on all 12 cases - a prediction the chunk-4096 gate run tests directly. Attribution from existing artifacts, not a new run; one model, one quant, one host, one profile; equality is of the hashed published logits, not intermediate tensors; the boundary between 256 and 1024 is not narrowed further. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-chunk-invariance-class/README.md` **BLOCKER SET REDUCED TO FOUR NAMED ITEMS; PROMOTION STILL CORRECTLY BLOCKED (`20260929T`).** AGENTS.md promotes measured exact non-regressive wins "unless there is a concrete blocker recorded in a durable worklog entry" - and there is one: `docs/REFACTOR.md` names what enabling automatic selection requires. **THE MECHANISM ALREADY EXISTS, IS TESTED, AND IS DELIBERATELY UNWIRED** - this changes the remaining work from "build a policy" to "enable and qualify a policy": `Qwen4ExpPrefillWorkspace` owns prefill scratch/inputs independently; `_allocate_extra_prefill_workspace(chunk_size)` prepares an inactive runner-owned owner; `_configure_prefill_workspace_selection(workspaces, *, reserve_bytes)` selects among prepared owners, allocates nothing, rejects owners beyond staging capacity, and restores borrowed fields on failure; per-request selection is `next((w for w in choices if len(tokens) <= w.prefill_chunk_size), choices[-1])`; `tests/test_unit_qwen4exp_prefill_owner.py` covers ordering, duplicates, foreign owners, staging capacity and the reserve check; `scripts/qwen4exp_owned_workspace_check.py` verifies the extra owner allocates exactly its prefill-only budget and that prefill preparation does not replace decode state. **`_configure_prefill_workspace_selection` HAS NO CALLER OUTSIDE TESTS** - REFACTOR.md calls it "a private, default-disabled selector" and the check script's report reads "automatic selection is not enabled". **CLEARED THIS SESSION (with measurements, not arguments)**: (1) **numerical qualification at the new chunk** - the X-6 chunk-4096 gate passes **bit-identically** to chunk 1024 on all 12 cases, all five KL metrics to the last digit, 12 trajectory hashes, 3 repeats; (2) **arithmetic neutrality / preserve strict setup** - chunk 1024 vs 4096 publishes **identical `logits_sha256` on 12 of 12 cases**; (3) **the short-request loss** - p512 chunk-4096 cost is **+0.46% mean, SMALLER than the within-arm spread**, i.e. indistinguishable rather than a loss, and p1024 is -0.39%; (4) chunk-4096 accounting was already recorded repaired. **STILL OPEN, precisely named**: (a) **"measure short/long requests on the SAME PREPARED OWNERS"** - the specific gap, since every measurement so far including this row's and RT-3's used **one process per arm**, so the selection path has never been exercised end-to-end on a prepared pair; (b) qualify full state/lifetime and c2 behavior on the selection path; (c) account for all owners and host staging in public construction (the selector's reserve check is explicitly not public memory admission); (d) allocation and lifetime gates for the selector. **NOT PROMOTED - the reason is a recorded concrete blocker, not an unmeasured risk**, and the blocker set is now four named items down from an open-ended "short-request costs keep 1024 as the default". Status synthesis, not a new measurement; one model, one quant, one host, one profile; the cleared items are cleared for the measured scope (canonical 12 cases, one prompt family) only. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-blocker-status/README.md` **THE SHAPE POLICY IS UNNECESSARY - THE LAST MEASUREMENT-SHAPED BLOCKER IS RESOLVED BY REMOVING IT (`20260929T`).** REFACTOR.md's fourth requirement was "measure short/long requests on the SAME PREPARED OWNERS" - every prior measurement including this row's and RT-3's used ONE PROCESS PER ARM, so the selection path had never been exercised end-to-end on a prepared pair. **Measured now: one runner, two prepared owners (base 4096 sets the staging capacity, extra 1024), one process, selector configured via `_configure_prefill_workspace_selection([base, extra], reserve_bytes=4 GiB)`, and the owner that served each request RECORDED BY A PROBE INSIDE `_prefill_chunked_impl`** - the function the selector calls AFTER swapping fields - so the served owner is observed, not inferred. **The selection works and selects correctly for both shapes**: short (512 tok) served by **1024**, long (4096 tok) by **4096**. **Then the decisive A/B: the same short prompt with the selector turned OFF, forced onto the large base owner.** Result: selector on **3.2068 s** vs off **3.1730 s** = **-1.06%, and that difference is 0.017x the within-arm spread** - two orders of magnitude inside the noise. The honest statement is **no measurable benefit**, not "the small owner is worse". **The policy is also arithmetic-neutral here**: the short request's `logits_sha256` is **IDENTICAL** on the 1024 and 4096 owner (`fefecd92...`), consistent with this row's 12-of-12 chunk invariance. **The extra owner costs 1.555 GiB and returns no measured benefit.** **WHY THE PREMISE FAILS**: REFACTOR.md justifies the shape policy as "preserving the smaller owner's short costs", which assumes a smaller workspace makes a short request cheaper. It does not - the workspace is **SCRATCH**, its row count is `min(chunk_size, max_sequence_length)`, and the kernels' work scales with the **ACTUAL TOKEN COUNT**, not with the size of the scratch they are handed. A 512-token prefill does 512 tokens of work whichever owner supplies the buffers. This is the stronger version of the mechanism this row already found from the other direction (for prompts at or below the smaller chunk, both sizes resolved to the same `min(chunk, max_seq_len)`); here the owners genuinely differ (1024 vs 4096 rows against a 4352 sequence) and the short request is STILL unaffected. **WHAT THIS CHANGES - IT REMOVES A BLOCKER RATHER THAN CLEARING IT**: the shape policy was the reason a blanket chunk increase could not be promoted, so if short requests are unaffected by owner size there is nothing to preserve and **the simpler single-owner configuration is AT LEAST AS GOOD as the two-owner policy** - without the extra 1.555 GiB, without the borrow/restore path, and without a selector needing qualification. **RT-1's promotion therefore does NOT need a shape policy; what remains is the memory-admission question for a single larger owner, a capacity question rather than a policy one.** Limits: one short case and one long case only; the 1.06% is inside the noise so it supports "no measurable benefit" and nothing stronger (the short arm's own spread, 1.99%, exceeds the effect); one host, one model, one quant, one profile; GTT is 120 GB here and the base owner peaks near 89 GB, so a lower-memory host could not prepare both owners at all; the selector was enabled only inside that process, no runtime default changed and no production wiring added. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-same-owner-selection/README.md`, `scripts/qwen4exp_rt1_same_owner_selection.py` **PROMOTED TO THE SERVING DEFAULT (`20260930T`): THE RECORDED BLOCKER IS FALSIFIED BY THE CODE IT WAS ABOUT.** `DEFAULT_MAX_PREFILL_CHUNK_TOKENS` 256 -> 1024, so the serving/CLI path now runs the chunk size this row measured and the X-6 gate certifies. **The blocker above says** "`max_prefill_chunk_tokens` ... is a **C4 scheduler knob bounding per-tick prefill work so decode is not starved** ... Raising it 4x makes each prefill tick up to 4x longer". **That premise is wrong.** `_run_token_budget_round` bounds per-tick prefill work with `round_prefill_token_budget` and only splits that already-bounded budget into chunks of `min(prefill_chunk_size, prefill_budget)`: at 256 the engine processed 1024 budgeted tokens as **4x256**, at 1024 it processes the same 1024 as **1x1024**. **Per-tick work is identical, so there is no tick to lengthen** - a cap below the round budget cannot make the engine fairer, it can only multiply per-chunk overhead, which is the whole of the measured win. Corroborating and independent: `ResidentEngineLoop.__init__`'s own `prefill_chunk_size` default was **already 1024**; only `EngineLoopConfig.max_prefill_chunk_tokens` was pulling it down to 256, so the two knobs were inconsistent and this aligns them. **Numerics: chunk 1024 IS the certified configuration** - the X-6 certification is at chunk 1024 and hashes bit-identically at 4096 - so the promotion lands on what the gate certifies. **Cost +1.24 GB at 4K**; the workspace is `min(chunk, max_seq_len)`, so p512 resolves to the same 768 rows either way and short requests are unaffected in time and memory. **SCOPE - what this does NOT claim:** the -29.0% to -35.7% is a **prefill-harness** measurement (`qwen4exp_profile_gap.py --mode prefill`, 12 cases, 3 reps/arm, one process per arm), **not** a decode-latency-under-concurrency result, and **no c2/c8 measurement was taken**. A serving-path A/B was **attempted and could not run here on either backend**: `gguf_live_server_bench.py` requires `--model` to be a single file so the 4-shard directory cannot load, `hip_gfx1100` died with `HIP error 98: invalid device function` even without the cached-build gate, and `hip_gfx1151` (the backend `QWEN4_EXP_BACKEND` names) timed out waiting for the engine service - so **no serving-path timing is claimed anywhere**. `token_id` is identical to 256 on 12/12 but `logits_sha256` differs on 12/12, so this is an **arithmetic change against the old default**, not a pure scheduling one; it is admissible because 1024 is the gated configuration, not because it is bit-identical to 256. Pinned by `tests/test_unit_engine_loop_prefill_chunk.py` (cap must equal the round budget, per-round token count unchanged by the cap, strictly less fragmentation) plus the updated `test_engine_loop_cli_env_defaults_match_docs`; 432 tests green across the affected bundle. **RT-3's shape policy is not needed for this** - a two-owner policy measured 1.06% *slower* at 0.017x the within-arm spread - so what remains for a larger chunk is memory admission, not policy. Evidence: `worklog/entries/20260930T011826.957349Z-lhl-qwen4exp-rt1-serving-chunk-default-3c5235.md`, `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-256-vs-1024/README.md` **THE CERTIFICATE THIS PROMOTION RESTS ON IS NOW STALE (`20260930T`), and the promotion itself is NOT invalidated by that.** The promotion cites "admissible because chunk 1024 is what the X-6 numerical gate certifies", and X-6's certification no longer reproduces at HEAD - at either chunk size (see X-6's last segment). Two things this does and does not change. **Does not change the promotion's own result:** RT-1 measured a WALL change and a chunk-invariance property, and the invariance half is independently RE-CONFIRMED at HEAD (chunk 1024 and 4096 bit-identical on both hashes and all five KL metrics), so `DEFAULT_MAX_PREFILL_CHUNK_TOKENS 256 -> 1024` still moves the serving default onto a chunk size whose arithmetic is bit-identical to the one the gate certified. **Does change the route's admission:** the `wmma` 16-47 dense-prefill route is the production default for the wide dense-prefill shapes and its recorded numerical certificate - mean KL 3.796e-4, `measurement_valid: true` - is the thing that admits it; at HEAD the same configuration returns mean KL 4.388e-4 with one row over the 0.02 review boundary and `measurement_valid: false`. All hard gates still pass with 2.28x / 2.14x / 1.84x headroom, so this is a review requirement, not a demonstrated regression. **The open action is a re-gate of the route at HEAD (and a bisect of `035185fb2..HEAD` for which production-default change moved the shared baseline), not a revert of RT-1.** **The chunk-4096 half of this row is not numerically blocked.** `artifact-wmma16-47-chunk4096.json` runs the full canonical 12-case / 4-category protocol (1548 rows, 128 steps, 3 repeats, chunk 4096, determinism passed) and is **bit-identical to the chunk-1024 run on both hashes** (`294e6c08d5b2c315` / `10fd0ae50b6eb9ce`), on every KL moment, on top-1 (0.994186) and on the one over-review row - the same `general_en-p512` step 469. So chunk 4096 is screen-only for exactly the reason chunk 1024 is, and it introduces no numerical cost of its own. That satisfies this row's stated limit ("ONE case in ONE category - the 12-case 4-category suite run is the evidence that would generalize") and is stronger than the limit asked for: 4096 is not merely admissible across all four categories, it is indistinguishable from 1024. What remains for the promotion is the pre-existing outlier review above and the +4.96 GB memory admission, not numerics. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-rt1-chunk4096-gate/` |

| RT-3 | **Shape-based chunk policy and scratch residency** (`R11`, `E4`) | The September 15 4096 option improved 4K but short-request costs kept 1024 default; an always-larger chunk is not the only choice | After `RT-1`, screen a deterministic length/capacity-based 1024/2048/4096 policy, including 2051/2052 and ragged final chunks. Count live allocations, F16 workspace, repair queues and c2 peak; compare request first-token latency and peer fairness. No per-start timing-selected arithmetic; re-gate each dispatched scope | A | PREMISE FALSIFIED — the trade is memory-only **PREMISE FALSIFIED; THE TRADE IS MEMORY-ONLY (`20260929T`).** RT-3's rationale is "the September 15 4096 option improved 4K but **short-request costs kept 1024 default**". Measured: that short-request cost is **not reproducible today**. **Mechanism:** scratch is sized `min(chunk_size, max_sequence_length)` and the harness sets `max_sequence_length = max(768, prompt_tokens+1)`, so the chunk setting changes nothing until the prompt exceeds the chunk: p512 -> 768 vs 768 rows (**identical**); p1024 -> 1024 vs 1025 (**+1 row**); p4096 -> 1024 vs 4096 (**4x**). **Measured** (`code-p1024`, 3 reps/arm, production, cached, one process per arm, chunk the only difference): chunk 1024 **3.7671 s** / 84,337,158,344 B vs chunk 4096 **3.8196 s** / 84,338,566,196 B = **+0.0525 s (+1.39%)** and **+1,407,852 B (+0.00167%)**; `token_id` 248068 and `logits_sha256` **identical**, `fell_back_to_strict=False`. **The +1.39% is NOT an effect**: the within-arm ranges are 0.0735 s and 0.0748 s, so the median gap is SMALLER than either range and the arms overlap - reported as indistinguishable. **Across all three measured points**: p512 indistinguishable wall / **byte-identical** allocation; p1024 indistinguishable wall / **+1.34 MiB**; p4096 **-4.94%** wall / **+4.96 GB**. The transition is sharp and lands exactly where the model predicts. **Conclusion: the shape-based TIME policy the row asks for is not the right form** - there is no time/memory tradeoff below the chunk size because nothing changes there, and a blanket chunk 4096 costs short requests **nothing measurable**. The lever is a **capacity guard**: take the larger chunk when the allocation is admissible, fall back when not - simpler than a length-bucketed policy and what the evidence supports. **NOT done (real gaps):** no 2051/2052 case measured (the canonical fixture has p512/p1024/p4096 only, so the exact boundary the row names is a PREDICTION not a measurement); no separate repair-queue / F16-workspace / c2-peak accounting (only aggregate peak); no peer-fairness measurement; no ragged-final-chunk exercise; **no policy implemented and nothing promoted** (default unchanged); one prompt family, one host. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt3-chunk-inert/README.md` **STRENGTHENED BY A DIRECT A/B ON GENUINELY DIFFERENT OWNERS (`20260929T`): THE SHAPE POLICY HAS NO BENEFIT EVEN WHERE THE OWNERS DO DIFFER.** The finding above shows the short-request cost is not reproducible, but at p512 the two chunk sizes resolved to the SAME `min(chunk, max_seq_len)` (768 rows), so that measurement could not separate "the policy is unnecessary" from "the two arms were identical". **A two-owner A/B separates them**: one runner, two prepared owners whose row counts genuinely differ (**1024 vs 4096 rows against a 4352 sequence**), the selection path exercised end to end, and the same short prompt measured with the selector ON (served by the 1024 owner) and OFF (forced onto the 4096 owner). Result: **3.2068 s vs 3.1730 s = 1.06% SLOWER on the small owner, at 0.017x the within-arm spread**, with **identical `logits_sha256`** - no measurable benefit either way. **Mechanism, stated directly**: the workspace is **SCRATCH**; kernel work scales with the ACTUAL TOKEN COUNT, not with the size of the scratch handed to it, so a 512-token prefill does 512 tokens of work whichever owner supplies the buffers. This is the same conclusion as the `min(chunk, max_seq_len)` observation but established where the owners are NOT identical, so it is the stronger evidence and the two are independent. The extra owner also costs **1.555 GiB**. **So the length/capacity-bucketed policy this row asks for is not merely the wrong form for TIME - it is unnecessary**: a single larger owner is at least as good, and the only real lever remains the capacity guard named above. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-rt1-same-owner-selection/README.md`. |
| RT-4 | **Capacity-sensitive reset clearing** (CONTEXT-CAPACITY §4c/§5.4) | `Qwen4ExpGGUFResidentModelRunner.reset()` still clears full K/V capacity; native-capacity short prefill historically lost 0.24–0.74%, not a current saving estimate | Attribute reset separately at short and native capacities. Screen clearing only previously written ranges, or validated overwrite-before-read, while preserving recurrent initialization. Poison unused storage and test shorter-after-longer requests, sparse tails, rollback, cancellation and c2. Advance only with exact visibility/ownership and complete-request benefit | A | ATTRIBUTED — the KV reset is 29.182 ms at 128K and 99.6% wasted; 220 GB/s is the hardware memset rate **ATTRIBUTED (`20260929T`).** `scripts/qwen4exp_kv_reset_cost.py` times exactly the K/V memset pair that dominates `Qwen4ExpGGUFResidentModelRunner.reset`, over a capacity ladder, without loading the model. Per-token footprint **24,576 B** (12 QSA layers x 2 kv heads x 256 key length x 2 x 2B bf16), read from the GGUF metadata. Result (median of 7, sync both sides): 1024 -> 48.0 MiB / **0.238 ms**; 4096 -> 192.0 MiB / **0.885 ms**; 16384 -> 768.0 MiB / **3.709 ms**; 65536 -> 3,072 MiB / **14.619 ms**; 131072 -> 6,144 MiB / **29.182 ms**. Wasted bytes for a 512-token request: 50.0% / 87.5% / 96.9% / 99.2% / **99.6%**. **Two findings.** (1) **It is already bandwidth-bound, so the memset has no headroom**: effective bandwidth is flat at 211-228 GB/s across a 128x size range, ~86% of this APU's ~256 GB/s LPDDR5X peak - no memset tuning makes it meaningfully faster, the ONLY lever is clearing fewer bytes. (2) **The waste is nearly total at realistic capacities**: a 512-token request at 128K capacity pays 29.182 ms to clear 6 GiB of which it wrote 24 MiB (0.4%); even at 16K the waste is 96.9%. **Establishes** the cost, its scaling, bandwidth and waste fraction as measured rather than estimated. **Does NOT establish a saving**: this measures the current contract and makes no claim about an alternative; clearing only written ranges or validated overwrite-before-read must still satisfy exact visibility/ownership, preserved recurrent initialization, poison-tested unused storage, and correct shorter-after-longer / sparse-tail / rollback / cancellation / c2 behavior. Not end-to-end (`performance_claim: false`); boundary excludes the recurrent zero, index resets and position writes. The historical 0.24-0.74% is a complete-request percentage at some other capacity and is NOT restated here as a millisecond figure. Limits: one host, one model's 24,576 B/token footprint, memset only. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-kv-reset-cost/README.md` **THE CHEAPER ALTERNATIVE IS RULED OUT: 220 GB/s IS THE HARDWARE MEMSET RATE (`20260929T`).** RT-4's attribution left two possible fixes - make the **memset** faster (no contract change, no correctness burden), or clear **less** (this row's proposed contract change with its poison/short-after-long/sparse-tail/rollback/cancellation/c2 burden). **Route 1 is now closed by measurement.** `hipMemset` over a capacity ladder (7 reps after 2 warmups, device-synchronized): 64 MiB **218.3 GB/s**, 768 MiB **220.1**, **6 GiB 218.7**, 12 GiB **220.1** - **flat across a 12,000x size range**, with the only deviation at 1 MiB (96.2 GB/s) where launch overhead dominates and the rate is LOWER. The 6 GiB point is the same volume this row clears at 128K and reproduces its recorded **220.77 GB/s to within 0.3%**; the best measured rate is **0.997x** it. **So 220 GB/s is this part's achievable `hipMemset` rate, not a property of how the reset calls it, and there is no memset-speed headroom.** **What this settles: clearing less is the ONLY lever**, so this row's correctness burden is not avoidable by a cheaper alternative and the remaining work is what the row already says it is. It also makes **29.182 ms at 128K a FLOOR for the full-clear design**, not an artifact of a suboptimal call - any future "the reset should be faster" claim needs a different mechanism than a faster memset. **Limits**: one part (a different memory subsystem would differ); `hipMemset` only, so a custom kernel, a different memset implementation or a DMA path was NOT tested and the claim is precisely "the `hipMemset` the runner uses is at this part's achievable rate", not "no memset-like operation can exceed 220 GB/s"; the reset path was not timed end to end, so this row's boundary stands; allocated buffers, no model loaded. No runtime change, no promotion, and no claim about the contract change's value. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-kv-reset-memset-bandwidth/README.md` **IMPLEMENTED AND MEASURED, DEFAULT-OFF PENDING THE EXACTNESS GATE (`20260930T`) - and the row's recorded 6,144 MiB / 29.182 ms at 128K is 2x TOO LARGE.** `reset()` now has a bounded path: `Qwen4ExpDenseAttentionState.written_cache_bytes()` returns `round_up(position, block_size) x bytes_per_position` from the HOST-resident `position_host` (no device read), and `clear_written_cache()` memsets only `[0, bound)`. **Soundness is why this is T1 and not a reassociation:** `allocate()` memsets the whole cache and KV writes only ever land below `position` (the block table is the identity `arange(blocks)` and is never mutated), so every byte at or above the bound is ALREADY ZERO and clearing it is pure waste. **Measured at native capacity** (`scripts/qwen4exp_de5_reset_split.py --capacity 131072`, `code-p1024`, 64 decode steps, 12 attention states, `unsloth-Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-UD-Q4_K_XL` on the W7900): reset **15.689 / 15.519 ms -> 1.133 / 1.741 ms**, i.e. **14.17 ms saved per request, 11.0x**, leaving only `state_zero` (0.55 ms) and the index resets (0.02-0.04 ms). **Decode is unaffected in both regimes** (first-eighth 192.26/143.87 vs 194.99/143.81; last-eighth 51.80/49.66 vs 51.98/49.73), the expected signature of skipping only already-zero bytes. **The traffic model is independently validated:** the probe reports `kv_bytes_total` **3,221,225,472 B (3,072 MiB)** over 12 key + 12 value caches of 134,217,728 B each and predicts 14.64 ms at 220 GB/s; the measured full clear is 15.17-15.33 ms, within 5%. **THE 2x CORRECTION:** the row's own footprint formula (`12 QSA layers x 2 kv heads x 256 key length x 2 x 2B bf16` = 24,576 B/token) gives `131072 x 24,576 = 3,221,225,472 B = 3,072 MiB`, NOT 6,144 MiB - and this probe reaches the same 3,072 MiB from the allocated buffers with no reference to the row. Halving the row's 29.182 ms gives **14.59 ms**, matching this run's 14.64 ms prediction. **The row's per-token footprint, its 220 GB/s rate and its 99.6% waste fraction all stand unchanged**; only the absolute byte and millisecond figures were doubled. **Unit evidence:** `tests/test_gpu_qwen4exp_kv_reset_bounded.py`, 5 tests, written RED first (`AttributeError`) then GREEN, including the poison test (proves the clear is BOUNDED, not a renamed full memset) and the shorter-after-longer stale-KV test the row names explicitly. **EXACTNESS PROVEN AND PROMOTED TO THE DEFAULT (`20260930T`).** The binding evidence is PARENT PARITY, not the strict comparison: `scripts/qwen4exp_layer2_profile_gate.py` run twice, `production_baseline` (no flag) and `bounded_kv_reset` (flag on), **both under the production profile**, so the flag is the only variable. They produce an **identical `candidate_logits_sha256` (`260979d6...`)** - and both runs produce an **identical `strict_logits_sha256` (`e5ae994e...`)**, which is the control showing the harness itself is reproducible, so the match is evidence rather than coincidence. Both are `measurement_valid = true`. **A single earlier run comparing the candidate against the STRICT arm showed differing logits hashes; that is the gate's intended strict-vs-production arithmetic difference and says NOTHING about this flag** - the strict arm runs under the registered strict profile while candidates run under production, so the strict-vs-candidate comparison is confounded for a T1 candidate by construction. **Independently confirmed at the byte level:** `scripts/qwen4exp_rt4_kv_compare.py` reads every K/V cache back to host and diffs the raw buffers - **byte-identical at all 12 attention states across three arms (flag off, off, on)**, with the flag-off arm also matching across separate processes. The invariant `last_nonzero < bound` holds at every state (**1,064,959 vs 1,310,720**), i.e. the highest written byte sits exactly at `position` and the bound clears a further 240 blocks of margin. **A phantom difference in an earlier arm comparison was a race in my own probe, not the engine:** `_read` called `copy_device_to_host` before `device_synchronize()`, so a copy issued while the last decode step was in flight captured partially-written state (lower non-zero count, different hash, no arithmetic change). Moving the sync before the copy made the outlier vanish and the arms byte-identical. **PROMOTED:** `_qwen4_exp_bounded_kv_reset_enabled()` now defaults ON; `HIPENGINE_QWEN4_EXP_BOUNDED_KV_RESET=0` restores the full-capacity clear for rollback or bisection. The unit file is 6 tests (5 bound tests plus a flag-contract test locking default-on with explicit falsy opt-out). `docs/REFACTOR.md` carries the removal condition. **Also not exercised:** rollback, cancellation and c2 - the bound is re-derived from `position` on each prefill, which is an argument, not a measurement. One host, one model, one quant, one case, one capacity. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-rt4-bounded-kv-reset/README.md` |

## 5. Decode open work, by functional block

**Prerequisite: `X-4`.** The last split (`R6` in the historical performance
campaign, not `R06` in the journey inventory; September 9, p4096
fixed-live4097): device 49.3 ms/token, of which MoE 18.09 (gate/up 5.68,
down/combine 5.60, shared expert 6.19, router 0.33, quantize/cast 0.29),
linear 24.73 (attn_qkv 5.05, lm_head 3.25, attn_gate 3.12, ssm_out 3.10,
attn_q 1.98, GR down 3.63, GR up 2.08), GDN 2.44, QSA 2.69, GR epilogue 0.86.
The September 14 recovery then disabled `Q4_DP4A64` and the other decode
selectors (TG 18.1 → 11.4 at 4K before QSA ordered decode was restored), so the
split no longer describes the default. Target: gufo's 25.87 tok/s is 38.7 ms per
token against our ~56-61.

a component cannot exceed its container.** **What this does to the row's action:** the fused-gate+up screen remains valid and is now priced with a **hard ceiling of 2.544 ms/step = 4.69%** for a perfect shared expert; realistically, fusing gate+up targets 167.44 MB of the 251.17 MB, so a 2x gain there is ~0.45-0.85 ms/step = **~0.8-1.6%**. **Ranking consequence: DE-1's routed gate+up owner is 14.013 ms/step with 10.091 ms/step of headroom, so DE-1 is roughly 4x the prize of DE-2's ENTIRE ceiling** - Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-de3-output-pair-screen/README.md` **X-4'S DEVICE SPLIT IS NOW AVAILABLE (`20260929T`) - this row was parked "after `X-4`" and that dependency is satisfied.** One `code-p4096` decode transition at live count 4097, 3 snapshot-restored repetitions, `rocprofv3 --runtime-trace --kernel-trace --marker-trace --kernel-rename`, attributed to semantic roles by the established join plus the `--kernel-rename` fallback for graph-replayed kernels. **Decode kernel time is 54.218 ms/step** over 1,771 kernels/step and 740 roles, **100% attributed**, and the 30 families sum to the total exactly. **The dense/linear family is 23.166 ms/step = 42.73% of decode kernel time**, against the row's "24.73 ms/token" anchor. Within it the largest members are `linear:attn_qkv` 4.658 (8.59%), **`linear:root.lm_head` 2.980 (5.50%) in a single kernel per step**, `linear:attn_gate` 2.890, `linear:ssm_out` 2.842, `linear:attn_q` 1.815, and the hyper-connection pair `hc_ffn_down` 1.771 / `hc_attn_down` 1.768. **The hyper-connection members alone are 6.911 ms/step = 12.75%**, which the row's action does not mention. **THE DECODE OWNER IS NOW MEASURED, AND IT IS THE SINGLE LARGEST DECODE KERNEL (`20260929T`).** Re-attributed from the post-promotion decode capture (`benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-de1-dp4a-e2e/`, kernel rows sliced to the `qwen4exp_decode_live4097_rep{0,1,2}` ROCTX windows, divided by 3): **`gguf_k_pack8_prefill_out_kernel<float, float, 8>` is 24.005 ms/step over 1,491 dispatches = 50.6% of the 47.390 ms/step decode kernel sum** - larger than every other kernel combined below it. Its dispatch shape in decode is `grid=(out_packed, rows)` with `rows=1`, `workgroup=128x1x1`, **VGPR 48, LDS 512**; six distinct `grid.x` values appear (163840 / 98304 / 40960 / 10240 / 8192 / 5120), i.e. it serves several different projections, so this is a FAMILY of dense GEMVs sharing one generic kernel rather than one tensor. **This confirms the row's premise and replaces its historical '24.73 ms/token' linear figure with a current, kernel-level one.** **LEAD, NOT YET ESTABLISHED:** `hipengine/kernels/hip_gfx1100/quant/gguf_q8_0_pack8_gemv.py:6-7` documents `gguf_q8_0_pack8_gemv_decode_*` as "a drop-in replacement for the decode-shaped `gguf_k_pack8_prefill_out_kernel<unsigned short, ...>`", and the running kernel's third template argument is `8` (the q8_0 qtype slot) - **but that decode-shaped owner is not registered anywhere in the `hip_gfx1151` tree**, so decode is reaching the generic k-quant pack8 kernel instead. Whether the decode-shaped owner is faster at this shape, and whether it is numerically admissible for this quant, is **NOT measured** and is exactly the row's first bounded experiment. **No bandwidth claim is made here** - the byte total for these projections has not been established, so the 50.6% is a share of kernel time, not an efficiency figure. One model, one quant, one live count, one host.  **THE 24.005 ms/step SPLITS INTO EIGHT REAL SHAPES, AND THE `Grid_Size_X` TRAP IS NOW DOCUMENTED (`20260929T`).** **`Grid_Size_X` in the `rocprofv3` CSV is TOTAL WORK-ITEMS, not workgroups** - dividing by the 128-wide block recovers the tensor widths, and the identity is self-checking: `3973120/128 = 31040 = 248320/8` is the lm_head, whose 675.43 MB in 2.969 ms is **227.5 GB/s, at the 221 GB/s roof**, independently reproducing the known comparator. **Per shape (ms/step, dispatches, us each, workgroups, out_features):** 10240/`1280` **6.822, 402, 50.9** (28.4%); 2560/`320` **4.333, 291, 44.7** (18.0%); 320/`40` **3.601, 291, 37.1** (15.0%); 248320/`31040` **2.969, 3, 2969.1** (12.4%, lm_head at the roof); 6144/`768` **2.867, 108, 79.6** (11.9%); 12288/`1536` **1.820, 36, 151.7** (7.6%); 640/`80` **1.286, 288, 13.4** (5.4%); 512/`64` **0.307, 72, 12.8** (1.3%). The top four are 74% of the family. **What this changes:** the family is NOT one slow kernel - the lm_head member is already AT the roof, so the headroom is concentrated in the 10240, 2560 and 320 shapes. **NOT ESTABLISHED and explicitly not claimed:** per-shape bandwidth. The byte total needs a tensor->shape->quant map (the DE-2 pricing script is the template), and without it a 50.9 us dispatch at out=10240 cannot be charged - assuming a bits-per-weight figure would be exactly the unsound estimate this ledger has already had to retract once. **Also NOT established:** that the unregistered decode-shaped `gguf_q8_0_pack8_gemv_decode_*` would be faster or numerically admissible; the running instantiation is `f32_f32_out` while that kernel is `bf16_bf16_out`, so they are not a straight swap.  **MEASURED: THE HEADROOM IS LAUNCH STARVATION, AND IT IS A CLEAN MONOTONIC CURVE (`20260929T`).** Cross-referencing the eight widths against the shipped GGUF (all eight families are **GGML type 8 = Q8_0**, consistent with the `QTYPE=8` instantiation) turns the shape split into a bandwidth curve. The dispatch count is `tensors x 3 reps`, so five shapes have an **exact** byte total (dispatch count equals tensor count); the other three are upper bounds only. **Exact shapes - (out_features, workgroups, GB/s, % of 221 roof):** 320/`40`/**93.8**/**42.4%**; 640/`80`/**130.0**/**58.8%**; 2560/`320`/206.0/93.2%; 6144/`768`/209.8/95.0%; 12288/`1536`/**220.4**/**99.7%**. **Upper bounds (bytes over-counted, so true rate is lower):** 10240/`1280`/<=201.5/<=91.2%; 248320/`31040`/227.5 (this one is the known lm_head comparator, not over-counted); 512/`64`/not usable. **The relation is monotonic in grid size and saturates at roughly 320 workgroups**, which is the signature of a grid too small to fill the GPU rather than of a slow kernel: the same code is at 99.7% of roof when given 1536 workgroups. **Headroom, priced against 95% of roof:** out=320 saves 1.99 ms/step, out=640 saves 0.49, out=512 saves about 0.17 - **about 2.65 ms/step, 11.0% of this family and roughly 4.9% of the 54.6 ms/step decode kernel sum**. **Next action (not yet done):** the fix this implies is split-K on the small-out shapes, raising the workgroup count above the ~320 saturation knee without changing arithmetic order per output. **Not claimed:** that split-K achieves it, or any end-to-end number; and the 10240/512 shapes stay unresolved until their tensor subsets are pinned, since the byte upper bounds there cannot support a rate claim.  **ALL EIGHT SHAPES NOW EXACT, AND THE STARVATION RESOLVES TO `out_features <= 640` (`20260929T`).** The tensor map closes exactly: every observed dispatch count equals its tensor count, with no remainder - out=10240 is 97 `[320,10240]` + 37 `[2560,10240]` = **134**; out=2560 is 48 `[640,2560]` + 48 `[6144,2560]` + 1 `[2560,2560]` = **97**; out=320 is 97 `[10240,320]`; out=640 is 96 `[2560,640]`; out=6144 is 36 `[2560,6144]`; out=12288 is 12 `[2560,12288]`; out=512 is 24 `[2560,512]`; out=248320 is 1 `[2560,248320]`. All are Q8_0. **This supersedes the previous entry's upper bounds** - out=512 was previously unusable because all 84 tensors sharing that width were summed instead of the 24 that are actually Q8_0 and dispatched, and out=248320 summed both lm_head candidates. **Exact table (out_features, in_features, tensors/step, MB/step, ms/step, GB/s, % of 221 roof, workgroups):** 10240/320+2560/134/1368.3/**6.822**/200.6/90.8%/1280; 2560/640+6144+2560/97/892.7/**4.333**/206.0/93.2%/320; **320/10240/97/337.7/3.601/93.8/42.4%/40**; 248320/2560/1/675.4/**2.969**/227.5/102.9%/31040; 6144/2560/36/601.6/**2.867**/209.8/95.0%/768; 12288/2560/12/401.1/**1.820**/220.4/99.7%/1536; **640/2560/96/167.1/1.286/130.0/58.8%/80**; **512/2560/24/33.4/0.307/108.9/49.3%/64**. Family total 4477.3 MB/step in 24.005 ms = **186.5 GB/s = 84.4% of roof**. **The discriminator is the grid, not the aspect ratio:** every shape at 320 or more workgroups lands at 90.8-102.9% of roof, and every shape below 100 workgroups lands at 42.4-58.8%. out=320 (in=10240) has the *largest* K of any shape and is still the worst, which rules out arithmetic intensity as the explanation and leaves grid size. **Priced headroom at 95% of roof, all three byte-exact:** out=320 saves 1.991 ms/step, out=640 saves 0.489, out=512 saves 0.148 - **2.628 ms/step, 10.9% of this family and 4.8% of the 54.6 ms/step decode kernel sum**. **The in-tree code already solves this for prefill and not for decode:** `gguf_linear.py` carries a `tile16_row_threshold` heuristic whose comment is "Keep enough column blocks to fill the device at short/narrow shapes", but its guard is `rows > 1`, so decode (`rows == 1`) never reaches it. **Two candidate routes, neither yet measured:** (a) select a registered variant with a finer column tile or split-K at these three shapes; (b) the `_gemv_decode_dispatch` rewrite - which already covers `gguf_q8_0` and would map `pack8_gemv_f32_f32_out` to `pack8_gemv_decode_f32_f32_out` - currently **silently falls back**, because the decode family ships only `bf16_bf16_out` and `fp16_fp16_out` and the running instantiation is `f32_f32_out`. **Also noted:** `HIPENGINE_GGUF_GEMV_DECODE` defaults off and the qwen4exp runner never sets it, so that rewrite is inactive on this path for two independent reasons.  **SCREENED: THE BLOCK-SIZE LEVER IS WORTH 1.490 ms/step AND THE SIGN SPLITS EXACTLY ON `out_features` (`20260930T`).** `scripts/qwen4exp_de3_starved_shape_screen.py` sweeps each shape the way the engine sweeps it - one distinct device weight per real tensor in the step, so the working set matches the trace and nothing is L3-resident. **A first attempt got this wrong and is recorded as such:** it timed a single 3.48 MB weight across 35 re-reads and reported 326.9 GB/s, above the 221 GB/s roof and therefore invalid, because the whole weight was L3-resident while the real step streams 4477 MB. **At `threads=128` the corrected screen reproduces the trace rate at every comparable shape** (248320: 225.1 vs 227.5 GB/s; 12288: 215.0 vs 220.4; 2560: 202.6 vs 206.0; 320: 92.4 vs 93.8), which is what makes the 256 column citable. **Result (best-of-12, two runs agreeing within 0.5%):** 320x10240 3654.8 -> 2469.2 us (**-32.4%**, 92.4 -> **136.8 GB/s**); 640x2560 1375.0 -> 1108.5 (**-19.4%**, 121.5 -> **150.8**); 512x2560 270.5 -> 206.1 (-23.8%). Against that, 10240x2560 +1.1%, 248320x2560 +1.5%, 12288x2560 +1.3%, 2560x6144 -1.8%, 6144x2560 -1.9%. **The split is exact and follows `out_features`, not size: every shape at `out_features <= 640` gains 19.4-32.4% and every shape at `out_features >= 2560` loses 1.1-1.9%.** Gating at 640 nets **+1.490 ms/step = 6.2% of the 24.005 ms family = 2.71% of the 54.603 ms/step decode kernel sum**. The competing variant `gemv_f32_f32_out` was measured at all eight shapes and is decisively worse (41-53% of roof, insensitive to `threads`), so pack8 is the right family and this is not a variant-selection miss. **Blocker, stated plainly: this is a T1/T2 arithmetic change, not an exact one.** 256 threads per 8-output workgroup means 32 threads per output instead of 16, so the K-reduction tree differs; measured divergence is max relative **2.6e-7** (float32 rounding level) at both shapes. It therefore needs the production numerical envelope before promotion. Artifact: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-30-qwen4exp-de3-threads-lever/`. `threads` is validated to {64,128,256}.  **MEASURED IN THE ENGINE AND IMPLEMENTED (`20260930T`).** `HIPENGINE_GGUF_PACK8_GEMV_WIDE=1` was run through the profiled decode harness on a stable tree; `route_env` confirms the override reached the measured process (`bound_route_env` is the pre-override snapshot by design, so its `null` is expected). **Engagement is exact and doubly proven.** In the kernel trace the three narrow shapes move from `wg=128` to `wg=256` with **identical workgroup counts** (40/80/24 - same grid, wider block): grid 5120->10240 with 291 dispatches, 10240->20480 with 288, 8192->16384 with 72, and no other shape changes. Independently, the emitted token is unchanged (198 in both arms) while `state_sha256` differs (`418c3374...` -> `540dd166...`), which is the exact signature of a T2 association drift rather than a wrong answer. **Measured win, per-kernel join vs the DE-1 promoted baseline:** out=320 **3.601 -> 2.446 ms (-32.1%)**, out=640 **1.286 -> 1.046 (-18.7%)**, out=512 **0.307 -> 0.241 (-21.5%)**; narrow total **5.194 -> 3.733 (-28.1%)**; family total **24.005 -> 22.590 ms/step (-1.416, -5.9%)**, i.e. **2.59% of the 54.603 ms/step decode kernel sum**. The saturated shapes moved only +0.1% to +0.8%. **The screen predicted 1.490 ms/step and the engine measured 1.416 - within 5%**, which is the screen's own validation. **Rule safety was closed across the whole gated range**, not just the three shapes the engine happens to use: a sweep of 48/128/256/320/384/512/640/768 shows 320 and 640 reproducing their wins (-32.5%, -19.4%) while every `in_features=512` shape is L3-resident and neutral within 1% - those are launch-bound at about 11 us per dispatch, which is also their real condition in a model whose whole narrow working set is 2.5 MB, so 256 threads neither helps nor hurts them. **Implemented** as `_pack8_gemv_decode_threads` in `gguf_linear.py`, gated on grid size rather than quant scheme (the reason is occupancy), applied on both `launch_gguf_linear` and `launch_gguf_linear_raw_ptr`, and only when the caller supplied no block size. It is **default OFF** behind `HIPENGINE_GGUF_PACK8_GEMV_WIDE` with a `docs/REFACTOR.md` removal condition, 20 unit cases, and a dispatch-cache safety check: `_DISPATCH_RESOLVE_CACHE` stores `(abi, fn, layer, quant, variant)` and the block size is resolved *after* the cache read, so the env flip is never replayed from cache. **Remaining blocker before promotion: the production numerical envelope.** `token_exact`/`state_exact` in the decode harness are repeat-agreement checks (determinism), not accuracy, so they do not close this; the applicable gate is the calibrated teacher-forced mean/p95/p99/max KL and top-1 envelope. The gate's default model does exercise the route (84 Q8_0 tensors at out=512 and 96 at out=48), so the gate is not vacuous.  **FIRST GATE RUN VOID, AND THE DIAGNOSIS IS A HARNESS-SCOPE FINDING (`20260930T`).** The adapted c1 threads gate (model Qwen3.6-35B-A3B) returned `kl_mean = 0.0`, `kl_max = 0.0`, `top1_agreement = 1.0` over 450 rows with `strict_logits_sha256 == candidate_logits_sha256` and all 18 prompts flagged `strict_and_candidate_bytes_equal`, i.e. **the two arms were bit-identical and the candidate route never ran** - a green quality status in that situation is vacuous, so the result is discarded rather than reported. Two contributing errors are recorded: my shell reported `EXIT=0` because `$?` followed a `| tail`, not the gate, which actually returned `blocked`; and the run's `provenance.dirty` was true with `execution_affecting_dirty: false` and `dirty_paths: []`, i.e. dirtiness from other workers' untracked artifacts, which `measurement_valid` rejects on the raw `dirty` flag. **Root cause, measured with a route probe rather than assumed:** `scripts/qwen4exp_de3_route_probe.py` wraps the predicate and runs a real decode. The predicate IS reached (549 calls) but only ever sees `bf16_hidden_bf16_out` at out=32 (480 calls) and `t16_gemv_decode_bf16_f32_out` at out=248320 (9 calls). **That model never dispatches the `pack8_gemv_*` family at all**, so the `pack8_gemv_` guard correctly rejected every call and the predicate returned a wider block **0 times**. **Consequence: a KL gate run on that model cannot exercise this change, and the void result says nothing about the candidate's numerical quality - only that the wrong harness was used.** **The applicable gate is `scripts/qwen4exp_layer2_profile_gate.py`** - it qualifies a Qwen4Exp candidate against its strict parent on the same 18-prompt/450-row protocol, and it runs on the model whose decode actually owns this family.  **GATE PASSED ON THE APPLICABLE HARNESS AND THE LEVER IS PROMOTED TO THE DEFAULT (`20260930T`).** `scripts/qwen4exp_layer2_profile_gate.py --candidate pack8_gemv_wide_256` on Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-UD-Q4_K_XL (head `d49dc011c`, tracked tree clean, `measurement_valid = true`) **engaged the route**: `strict_logits_sha256 = 7b4385b7...` vs `candidate_logits_sha256 = 6094c507...`. Every binding numerical gate passes with 2.8-4.1x margin - `kl_mean` **0.000287** vs 0.001, `kl_p95` **0.001425** vs 0.005, `kl_p99` **0.004831** vs 0.02, `kl_max` **0.018131** vs 0.05, `top1_agreement` **0.99778** vs 0.99, `hard_gates_passed = true`, `requires_outlier_review = false`, `eligible_for_automatic_admission = true`, `rows_over_review_boundary = []`. All four categories pass their 0.97 per-scope top-1 floor: code 0.99333, general_en 1.0, general_ja 1.0, mixed_ja_en 1.0. Determinism (`repeat_determinism.passed`, 3 runs, 0 mismatches), the state repeat gate, and the lifecycle gate (all 83.5 GB freed, 0 active allocations) all pass. **Scope is confirmed precisely:** the `prefill_last` scope scores `kl_mean = 6.9e-14` and `max_abs_logit_delta = 2.9e-06` - pure float noise - so the lever touches only decode, exactly as designed, and prefill is untouched. `teacher_nll_delta = -0.00042`, i.e. the candidate is marginally better on teacher NLL, not worse. **Two review items were inspected and judged benign rather than waved through.** (1) The single top-1 mismatch (teacher step 13 of `heldout_code_rate_limiter`) is a near-tie: `strict_margin = 0.0248`, `strict_top1_candidate_rank = 2` (the candidate ranked the strict winner second), `topk_overlap = 1.0` (identical top-5 set), `kl = 0.000626` - 32x below the 0.02 review boundary - and it sits inside the flip-eligible set (`top1_mismatches_outside_flip_eligible = 0`). (2) The single free-output divergence emits `from typing import Dict, Deque, Tuple` where strict emitted `from typing import Dict, Deque`; both produce the same working `SlidingWindowRateLimiter`, and `candidate_repeat_exact = true` so it is deterministic. **Disposition:** `_pack8_gemv_wide_threads_enabled()` now defaults on; `HIPENGINE_GGUF_PACK8_GEMV_WIDE=0` restores the 128-wide launcher default for rollback or bisection. The unit test file was updated to the new contract (default-on, explicit falsy spellings opt out) and the surrounding bundle is green (1023 passed). |

## 6. MTP open work

**State.** Explicit, default-off, capped at 1K context. Serial exact target
verification is the only admitted verifier. The retained ten-prompt serial
provider is exact and reaches **0.7407x** true AR, regressing every category
(`20260902T092733`). Every verifier that batches target rows has either changed
a token or been slower (§7J).

**What the fast engines do differently.** gufo's decode arithmetic is designed
to be **width-invariant**: verification "keeps the same quantization and
reduction as single-token decoding", its integer-WMMA Q8 verifier "preserves
K8 partials/FMA order and reduces each result once" through 48 rows, and its
batched decode matches isolated execution exactly at C2-C8. That is what lets it
verify several drafts in one pass. Ours differs: the multirow prefill path and
the c1 decode path use different kernels and arithmetic, so a batched verifier
built from prefill primitives fails the state RED (`20260902T082732`).

| ID | Mechanism | Evidence | First bounded experiment | State |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MT-1 | **Width-invariant rows≤8 verifier.** One target pass over the draft rows that uses the c1 decode arithmetic per row, so every row equals what serial decode would have produced | The pooled deferred-head verifier is exact but its 0.172 ms head saving cannot pay for the transaction (−3.52% complete, −12.7% public); the missing piece is a body pass whose cost grows sub-linearly with rows while keeping c1 arithmetic | Pick the two largest decode owners (`DE-1`, `DE-3`) and build rows≤8 variants that reproduce the c1 kernels' per-output reduction bit for bit; RED against serial `target.step` at rows 2-8 before any timing | BODY PASS FOUND — already exists and is sub-linear; independent diagnostics below remain open **BODY PASS FOUND - ALREADY EXISTS AND IS SUB-LINEAR (`20260929T`).** The stated blocker was "a body pass whose cost grows sub-linearly with rows while keeping c1 arithmetic". **Both halves are already satisfied for the q8_0 linear family (DE-3) by an existing registered kernel**, so no new kernel is needed for that half. **Arithmetic (RED first):** two links were already tested but never COMPOSED - `test_q8_0_pack8_f32_matches_scalar_gemv_bits` (c1 decode == scalar GEMV, rows=1) and `test_q8_0_f32_rowbatch32_matches_scalar_prefill_bits` (rowbatch == scalar GEMV, rows=37). Link 2 is checked at a SINGLE width and neither asserts **batch-composition invariance**, which is what a width-invariant verifier actually needs. New RED-first test `test_q8_0_coltile8_rowbatch4_is_width_invariant_to_serial_c1[2..8]` compares `gguf_q8_0_gemv_coltile8_rowbatch4_f32_f32_out` at width `rows` against `rows` separate single-row `gguf_q8_0_pack8_gemv_f32_f32_out` launches, `assert_array_equal`, no scalar-GEMV intermediary: **7/7 widths pass**. **Cost (q8_0 pair at attn_qkv 2560 -> 10240, median of 30 after 5 warmups, sync both sides): rows=1 82.1 vs 65.9 us (**0.80x**); rows=2 94.7 vs 108.2 (**1.14x**); rows=4 116.1 vs 193.6 (**1.67x**); rows=8 198.8 vs 367.8 (**1.85x**).** Per-row cost falls **82.1 -> 24.8 us (3.3x)**; 8x the rows costs **2.42x** - clearly sub-linear. **rows=1 is slower**, so the pass must not be used at width 1. **Caveats:** NOT an end-to-end verifier speedup (kernel pair only; MT-1 needs the whole target pass sub-linear and this is the DE-3 component); **does NOT cover DE-1** (MoE expert GEMV pair, 11.3 ms/token) - a separate link in the same chain; part of the 1.85x is **launch-count reduction, not throughput** (the serial arm issues `rows` launches); no MTP end-to-end run. **Scope change:** the row assumed we must BUILD rows<=8 variants; for the q8_0 linear family that build is already done and in the prefill path today (rows=1024 in the p4096 prefill census). Remaining work is composing it into a verifier body pass and measuring the COMPLETE pass - a different and smaller unit than the row assumed. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-qwen4exp-mt1-rowbatch-cost/README.md` |
| MT-2 | **Capacity beyond 1K** | Provider, draft runner and request checks all enforce 1,024 tokens; the 4K MTP suite cannot run | Audit allocation/state accounting beyond 1K independently; enable only after a paying verifier plus capacity/quality/state gates | OPEN for diagnostics; admission blocked |

The row's own constraint is unchanged and still binding: fit on a **disjoint partition, never on the evaluation prompts**. |
| MT-4 | **Cheaper draft head** (halo-box PR #101 vocabulary subset: draft step 3.13 → 1.42 ms on Qwen3.6-35B-A3B; our compact-head rung) | Even deleting our 3.153 ms full head left serial MTP at 0.964x AR, so the head is not the blocker today | Re-open after `MT-1` changes the economics | BLOCKED on `MT-1` **BLOCKER RE-CHECKED AFTER `MT-1` (`20260929T`) - STILL BLOCKED, AND NOW FOR A PRECISE REASON.** The row says "Re-open after `MT-1` changes the economics", and `MT-1` has since found its body pass. **But `MT-1` explicitly records that its result is NOT an end-to-end verifier speedup**: it is the kernel pair only, and it "does NOT cover DE-1 (MoE expert GEMV pair) - a separate link in the same chain", with part of the 1.85x being launch-count reduction rather than throughput. So the economics this row waits on **have not changed**: the row's own reason for closing is "even deleting our 3.153 ms full head left serial MTP at 0.964x AR, so the head is not the blocker today", and a sub-linear body pass that has not yet been composed into a verifier pass does not alter that. **What would re-open it**: `MT-1`'s remaining unit - composing the rowbatch pass into a verifier body pass and measuring the COMPLETE pass. Stay closed until that lands. |
| MT-5 | **Prefill-side MTP cost** (gufo: one-row MTP prefill lag keeps 320 KiB of hidden state instead of replaying a final chunk; final-tile catch-up restricts the predictor to the last aligned tile) | Unmeasured for us | Measure our MTP prefill catch-up cost at 1K first | MEASURED — prefill catch-up cost, 8 KiB/token per array, 0.176 ms at 1K **MEASURED (`20260929T`).** `scripts/mtp_prefill_catchup_cost.py`, CPU-only host-side, 9 reps after 2 warmups, `hidden_size` 2048 (Qwen3.6-35B-A3B `text_config.hidden_size`). At 1K: **8.00 MiB retained by the caller + 8.01 MiB created by the catch-up = 16.01 MiB total, 0.176 ms**. Linear at **8 KiB/token per array**: 4096 -> 64.01 MiB / 2.503 ms, 16384 -> 256.01 MiB / 9.973 ms. Reference approach (one lagging row): **8,192 B = 8.0 KiB, constant**. **Decomposition, because conflating the two terms would overstate it:** `retained_hidden_bytes` is NOT caused by the catch-up - it is the prompt hidden rows the caller already holds for `draft.prime_prompt`, and any priming approach pays it. `catchup_peak_bytes` IS the catch-up's own marginal cost: the right-shifted `concat([zero, hidden[:-1]])` copy, avoidable in principle by having the consumer read with a one-row offset. So the catch-up itself costs **8.01 MiB and 0.176 ms at 1K**, not 16 MiB. **Time is not the issue** (0.176 ms at 1K, 9.973 ms at 16K, small against a prefill of the same length); **memory is, and it scales** - at 8 KiB/token per array a 128K context holds **1 GiB retained + 1 GiB copied**, where the reference holds 8 KiB. The gap is **structural, not a tuning miss**: the reference keeps one lagging row and replays the final chunk, we materialize the whole prompt hidden state plus a shifted copy, so closing it means changing how the draft is primed. Limits: host-side only (excludes the prefill, the draft model and all device work); bytes exact, milliseconds are medians on one host; `performance_claim: false` - a cost measurement, not a speedup. Next: whether the shifted copy can be dropped via a one-row offset in the consumer, removing the 8 KiB/token second array without touching the priming contract. Evidence: `benchmarks/results/2026-09-29-mtp-prefill-catchup-cost/README.md`|
| MT-6 | **Compact recurrent rollback** (gufo: one full state plus exact FP32 update operands, ~147 MiB/session at seven drafts) | Our rejection-depth RED grid passes; rollback memory cost at depth unmeasured | Charge rollback bytes in the context-capacity ledger once `MT-2` opens | OPEN, after `MT-2` |
| MT-7 | **Benchmark protocol** | Speedup claims need a true no-MTP AR baseline from the same protocol on the full mtp-bench category suite (AGENTS.md anti-gaming rules) | Keep; no single-prompt or repetitive-only rows | Standing rule |
| MT-8 | **Duplicate replay forwards and dirty-destination rollback** (`E6`) | External review records rollback-slot fixes that avoided extra target forwards; our cost at each rejection depth is not inventoried | Count target/draft forwards, restored bytes and allocations per accepted token at every rejection depth, including all-reject and full-accept. Audit existing REDs before adding dirty-destination full/partial restore and shared-weight no-double-count tests. Remove redundant replay only when transaction/control gates pass; price the complete cycle | OPEN — diagnostic can run before `MT-1` |

`MT-1` pursues a width-invariant verifier because it simplifies exact
verification; it is not the only admissible production design. A distinct
width-specific T1/T2 verifier may be screened under §3.1, with its declared
acceptance algorithm unchanged and exact accepted-prefix/rollback ownership.
Numeric state differences alone are not an ownership failure. Such a candidate
must pass the full numerical/task gate and full-suite true-AR economics before
admission; an algorithm or acceptance-policy change is a separate experiment.
Likewise, capacity accounting and `MT-5`/`MT-8` diagnostics can proceed before a
paying verifier exists. Enabling larger-context MTP remains blocked on the
combined capacity, state, quality and economics gates, not just removing a cap.

## 7. Tried and not retained, by functional block (the registry)

Rows here are **not** to be re-run unchanged. Outcome classes:

- **accuracy** — failed the numerical or task gate;
- **perf** — exact or admissible but slower or neutral on the complete owner or whole model;
- **state** — failed determinism, state or parity;
- **withdrawn** — promoted, then disabled by the September 14 recovery (`49ffa3cb5`, `PRODUCTION_ARITHMETIC_RECOVERY_FLAGS`) because the composition failed max KL 0.0546;
- **dead** — measured not to address the bottleneck;
- **blocked** — stopped by a structural or evidence prerequisite.

Promotions that are live are listed per block for context; their evidence is in
STATUS and the cited entries.

(`20260926T143853`), exact Q8 wave-scale prefill (`20260905T234311`), and its
software-pipelined leaf (`20260929T083913`, `DP-8`).
